its mere difference is not malformed input.

An ordinary one-bit X action with no regions has domain t=0,1 and table
0→1, 1→0. Its full-D exhaustive check must receive only `proof`, whether
fixed_regions is absent or empty. This witness catches the first note's
vacuously true image-relative condition.

At A=16,W=8, with x width 10, t width 1 and f width 4, there are 15
varying bits and 524288 fixed image bits. Coverage against unrestricted D
is exactly 1/2^524288, never floating-point zero. Prepared cells remain
interface components even though the experiment fixes them.

## Recommended exact spec changes

Extend the §12.2 domain schema and the certificate label-request/checker
outcome enumerations; add a §0.2 K entry. Replace BOTH the introductory
"otherwise evidence" rule in §12.2 and K74's blanket strict-slice
classification with the exclusive classifier below. This is a proposed
in-version certificate extension; no instruction or domain D changes.

```text
A domain MAY contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}]. An absent
field is an empty list. name resolves by the existing component rules to
an in-role interface region, with precisely the subject's A and W.
Each resolved fixed region occurs exactly once and MUST NOT also occur
in domain.regions. A mut-role region cannot be fixed by this mechanism.

image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) raw bytes encoded as lowercase hexadecimal;
unused high bits in the final byte MUST be zero. Cell i consists of bits
[iW,(i+1)W) in the little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of the raw
bytes, not the hexadecimal text. The checker hashes independently and
uses those cells on every replay. Fixed cells participate in domain
validity and tuple/output checks, but each contributes one choice to n.
The full unrestricted |D| remains unchanged. This metadata introduces no
operation for inspecting unknown coherent state.

After every §12.1 replay obligation succeeds, an exhaustive
table/program/oracle claim receives exactly one outcome, in this order:
(1) proof iff its domain equals unrestricted D component by component;
(2) otherwise proof_relative_to_image iff fixed_regions is nonempty,
    every image binding is valid, every fixed region is accounted for,
    and every remaining component occurs exactly once with its full
    range (all interface bundles/cells, borrowed positions and prepended
    inherited controls included);
(3) otherwise evidence.
Successful sampled claims always receive evidence, including when their
distinct draws cover the whole domain. Malformed domains, stale hashes
and mismatches reject, rather than receiving any successful verdict.
Omission-at-zero remains a strict slice unless the original component
is thereby genuinely at its full range under the existing rules.

Coverage remains exact n/|D| against unrestricted D, with distinct-draw
coverage for sampling. The checker report identifies closure_hash,
claims-array index, invocation/context key, and ordered resolved
(region identity,A,W,image hash) bindings. A supplied label is a request,
not a producer assertion. Requested proof rejects unless the computed
outcome is proof; requested proof_relative_to_image rejects unless the
computed outcome is proof_relative_to_image. An evidence request may
accompany a stronger independently computed outcome, as before.

K75's host-limit handling remains: unchecked (host limit) discharges no
proof obligation and does not invalidate a legal domain or shrink it.
```

This deliberately preserves full-D K74 proofs and evidence for ordinary
strict slices. The sole new successful classification is a nonempty,
valid image binding with all other components fully quantified.

```text
A proof_relative_to_image theorem is conditional on the stated image.
Using it to discharge an executable invocation additionally requires
checked preparation/binding establishing that this in-region contains
exactly those classical bytes and is factorised from other systems at
entry, and that its exit image is restored. Establish preparation from
declared bytes and logical preparation operations, never inspection of
unknown coherent data. Without that preparation evidence the theorem
remains conditional and does not discharge the invocation.

This verdict cannot discharge an unrestricted borrow-frame, inverse-pair
or other full-D obligation, and cannot be reused for another image,
invocation, target realisation or schedule by discarding its bindings.
All §12.1 exact +1 basis-phase, full-tuple, clean-workspace and
external-reference obligations remain in force. A basis table cannot
prove a non-permutation realisation; its numerical/channel obligations
are unchanged. Target matching, resource accounting and error
composition are not relaxed by conditional correctness.
```

For fixed image I, write J_I for the image-preparation isometry including
prescribed clean workspace. The permutation assertion is `V J_I = P J_I`,
also after tensoring with identity on an arbitrary external reference.
When a process claim is needed, compare
`E_real ∘ Ad_J_I` and `E_ref ∘ Ad_J_I` with the complete output interface.
Controlled-unitary comparison retains a phase-sensitive bound on
`||U J_I − V J_I||`; equal uncontrolled channels alone cannot discharge it.
Numerical/channel acceptance procedures remain those of §12.1.

## Mixed fat object (bennett-4ht)

Add to §§9/10/12: "A native binary may carry VM objects only for marked
modules. The host launch manifest selects a VM entry and records the
classical image preparation/copy into its in-region, with resolved shape
and raw-byte hash. The certificate inventory is that entry's selected VM
closure and reached contexts, not the whole native executable. Image
preparation may come from host database construction or a declared
snapshot. Host bytes are bound to device cells using the declared packing,
not host pointer identity. Unmarked main, argv/envp, libc and unrelated
code remain native. The native comparison invokes the corresponding
materialized marked entry over the same image and varying domain; it
need not treat the entire binary as the oracle. Runtime shims and build
configuration are recorded as required by the North Star."

A preparation witness for this note is a host buffer `01 02` copied to
the selected two-cell VM in-region before entry and retained unchanged
through the oracle's return. Preparing an entangled region does not meet
that classical-image precondition. This says how a mixed object uses the
contract; a physical launch shim need not be implemented by this note.

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

| Item | Change and concrete witness |
|---|---|
| sol XV4-B01: new rule overlaps full-D proof and K74 strict-slice evidence | Replace both blanket rules with one ordered, exclusive classifier; require nonempty fixed_regions. Full-D X receives proof, the 64-tuple 0102 slice receives proof_relative_to_image with 1/65536 coverage, sampling receives evidence. |

The additional round-1 suggestions are retained: a complete oracle
relation, stale-versus-fresh image mutations, explicit preparation-isometry
comparison, and separation of requested labels from checker outcomes.

## Hashes, publication and consumers

Fixed images and requested labels change certificate bytes; assigned
verdicts and resolved bindings are checker-report data. Under an
authorized 0.9 K extension retaining the semantic envelope, §9.2 excludes
certificate sections from object_hash, so source/interface/closure/object
identities, targets, schedules and ref catalog remain unchanged. A full
version bump instead requires regenerating version-envelope identities
and dependent pins. This note authorizes neither publication choice.

Producers/checkers must publish schema and verdict vectors together;
legacy schemas must not silently treat the qualified spelling as proof.
Until acceptance retain current evidence labels and exact fractions.
The SQLite rung still requires unmodified SQLite, real SQL, quantum
addressing, all rows/targets at N≤2^10, Grover at N≤2^8 and the named-target
N=2^20 estimate. Conditional correctness does not replace those gates.
xv4 remains open for acceptance and implementation.

## Conformance vectors and verification

In addition to the three exclusive-verdict cases: swapping 0102 to 0201
while retaining the old digest, expected relation or preparation binding
rejects reuse. Updating all bindings permits a fresh check, never reuse
of the old theorem. Test hashing hex text instead of bytes, wrong A/W,
nonzero padding, duplicate region identities, omitted target/fault/dirty
ranges, partial controls, a requested unqualified proof and a mut-region
binding. Identical image strings for two distinct resolved region slots
remain separate bindings. Test outer controls 0/1, arbitrary dirty states
and external references; sampled full coverage remains evidence.

This exact finite witness checks hashes, the complete 64-tuples relations,
exclusive classification premises and coverage. It is not execution of a
new checker/schema, a SQLite run or council acceptance.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
import hashlib
import itertools

images = (bytes([1, 2]), bytes([2, 1]))
hashes = [hashlib.sha256(i).hexdigest() for i in images]
assert hashes == [
    'a12871fee210fb8619291eaea194581cbd2531e4b23759d225f6806923f63222',
    '25dfd29c09617dcc9852281c030e5b3037a338a4712a42a21c907f259c6412a0']
assert hashes[0] != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
rows = list(itertools.product(range(2), range(2), range(16)))
assert len(rows) == 64 and F(len(rows), 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
for image in images:
    for x, t, f in rows:
        payload = image[x] & 1
        after = (x, t ^ payload, f, image)
        assert (after[0], after[1] ^ payload, after[2], after[3]) == (x, t, f, image)
assert sum((images[0][x] & 1) != (images[1][x] & 1) for x, t, f in rows) == 64
assert all((images[0][x] & 1) == (1 ^ x) for x in range(2))
assert all((images[1][x] & 1) == x for x in range(2))
# Full-D X: empty image set cannot satisfy the qualified predicate.
full, fixed, remaining_full = True, [], True
assert full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
# Fixed-image slice: not full D, but every other component is full.
full, fixed, remaining_full = False, [hashes[0]], True
assert not full and bool(fixed) and remaining_full
# Omit a target range: neither proof predicate holds.
remaining_full = False
assert not full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
assert 2**16 * 8 == 524288
assert F(2**15, 2**(15 + 524288)) == F(1, 2**524288)
print('PASS xv4: two 64-tuple image relations; 64 swapped-image failures; exclusive proof predicates; exact coverage')
```

name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. # Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# xv4 — proof relative to a fixed classical region image

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-xv4`.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §5.2's D includes every value of every interface-region cell;
§12.2 K69–K74 assigns `proof` only for componentwise full D. A successful
exhaustive slice is `evidence`, even if every varying component of the
actual experiment was enumerated. Dialect 0.3 draft §§19.9/25.2 correctly
requests today's evidence label. The existing checker is not defective
for assigning it.

North Star §5.1 defines proof as exhaustive over a declared finite domain,
and §8 rung 4(a) explicitly fixes the database classically before entry
while varying all rows and all target values. There is a **certificate
scope/terminology mismatch**, not missing SQLite execution semantics.
Keeping an explicitly conditional proof restores that meaning without
claiming correctness for other databases. Shrinking the varying input,
fault, target, dirty, or inherited-control domain is not an acceptable
substitute; neither are SQLite source edits or a sampled row check.
The exact underlying machine capability is already present. Under the
rubric, judge the gap against the promised proof meaning, not as a
failure of existing K74 replay or of the rung-4 evidence gate.

## Alternatives and worked example

For a tiny oracle with one row-index bit, one target bit, four fault bits,
and one `in` region[1,8], the unrestricted D has 22 bits. Fix image bytes
`01 02`; enumerate all x,t,f values: 64 cases. That is fraction
`2^6/2^22 = 1/65536` of full D, so current K74 says evidence. All 64
cases nonetheless prove the oracle relation for this image if the
realisation is exact phase-free permutation code with full tuple checks.
An implementation that always substitutes bytes `01 02` agrees on this
slice and fails on `02 01`; the conditional proof must not certify it
for the latter image.

At the supplied rung sizes A=16,W=8, x has 10 bits, t one bit and f
four bits: 15 varying bits and 524288 fixed region bits. The fraction
is exactly `1/2^524288` when no further components are present. Do not
round it to zero. Prepared image bytes are a precondition, not absent
interface components.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Keep K74 unchanged | Valid current conformance, but a finite exhaustive fixed-image experiment keeps the evidence label; document the North Star terminology gap explicitly. |
| Call the slice an unqualified proof | Unsound promotion to all database images. |
| Conditional proof with checked binding (recommended) | Proves exactly the prepared-image relation and keeps unconditional K74 proof distinct. |
| Embed the database as a local region image | Can prove that specialized closure on its full external D; changes identity and preparation route, and does not relabel the original interface-region claim. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Extend §12.2 domain records and add a §0.2 K entry:

```text
A domain MAY additionally contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}].
name resolves to an in-role interface region by the existing component
rules. image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) bytes as lowercase hexadecimal;
unused high bits of the final byte, if any, MUST be zero. Cell i is bits
[iW,(i+1)W) of that little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of these
raw bytes, not their hexadecimal spelling. A fixed region must occur
exactly once, cannot also occur in domain.regions, and must have the
subject's exact A and W. The checker independently hashes the image and
uses its cells on every replay. Mut-role regions cannot use this binding.

An exhaustive table/program/oracle claim that succeeds on every element,
has every non-fixed component at its full range, and binds all remaining
components only by fixed_regions receives proof_relative_to_image.
The checker reports the region closure hash, claim index, invocation or
context key, and ordered (region identity,A,W,image hash) bindings with
that verdict. It also reports coverage against the original unrestricted
D. This is a proof only on the stated fixed-image subspace. Sampled
claims always remain evidence, including on that subspace. Unqualified
proof retains K74's full-D condition.

Use of proof_relative_to_image for an executable invocation additionally
requires a checked preparation/binding establishing that the in-region
has exactly that classical image and is factorised from other systems
at entry, and that the region restores it at exit. Preparation is checked
from the declared classical bytes and logical preparation operations,
not by inspecting unknown coherent data. Without this precondition the
claim remains a conditional theorem and cannot discharge that invocation.
This verdict cannot discharge a full-D borrow-frame, inverse-pair or
other unrestricted obligation, nor be silently reused for another image.
All phase, full-tuple, external-reference and invocation-binding
obligations of §12.1 continue to apply.
```

Extend the checker verdict enumeration to include the spelling above;
requested `proof_relative_to_image` must be checked just like K74's
requested labels. Non-permutation lowerings retain process/channel
obligations; this is not permission to certify them from a basis table.
Where such a conditional process claim is needed, explicitly compare
the channel after the same image-preparation isometry on both sides.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source closure, interface, target or catalog bytes change. The
certificate's fixed image and conditional verdict alter its own bytes;
an image change invalidates the binding even if the region hash is the
same. Plugin, checker and rung gates must retain the qualified verdict
and hash visibly. Schemas must not silently accept it as old `proof`.
Until acceptance consumers must keep current evidence labels and exact
fractions; the new spelling is only proposed here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the 64-case tiny example; its raw-byte hash is computed in
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). Change one
image byte, swap cells, change W, hash the hex text, omit a target/fault
range, or request unqualified proof: reject that requested promotion.
Sampling all 64 cases remains evidence. Two identical image strings at
different resolved region identities are separate bindings. Test outer
control 0/1 and dirty systems in full; a preparation entangled with a
reference does not meet the fixed-image premise. These are proposal
vectors; neither a rung-4 gate nor a new checker test was run here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate clarification/extension** if the council
accepts a new qualified outcome; preserve every old K74 label. Add it to
schema and checker vectors together before revising rung acceptance.
The broader North Star rung remains unmodified SQLite, real SQL,
quantum-addressed memory, all rows/targets at N≤2^10, Grover at N≤2^8,
and the N=2^20 resource estimate. xv4 stays open for that decision.

995:| `qinplace` | `qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins..) (mem..) (args..); qfree t; qfree f`, so x ← g(x; ins, mem, args); these are its generated operations, in this order (§11). **Inverse-pair obligation**: under the tuple of `tk`, the call's `args` values and the tuples of the tokens among them, on every basis value of x, of the `ins` wires and of the `mem` cells, both oracles are fault-free and g(·; ins, mem, args) and ginv(·; ins, mem, args) are mutually inverse permutations of the n-bit encoding. It is carried by an `inverse_pairs` entry (§12.3) of the certificate record of the region whose body contains the `qinplace`; an entry `{g: G, ginv: H}` covers both `qinplace @G @H` and `qinplace @H @G` (its claim checks both compositions). An entry **covers** a `qinplace` when its `budget` is componentwise ≤ the tuple of `tk` (success at a tuple implies the same result at every larger one, §5.4), every token-typed argument's tuple equals the entry's `arg_tokens` tuple for that argument (equality: a nested oracle's outcome may change with its budget), and every other `args` value lies in its claim's `args` domain. Its claim is an `exhaustive` claim with `expected: "inverse_pair"`, which the executor verifies as a checker does (once per closure hash, before first use), or a `structural` `inverse_pair_library` claim, which covers when the named claim of the linked library's certificate is such a claim for the same closure hashes of g and ginv (no re-execution; a library claim without an `args` domain covers every value; its proof or evidence label is the library claim's); a `sampled` claim never covers. Before any effect of a CC/R region call, every `qinplace` the callee executes (transitively; its token and `args` are resolved from the call's classical arguments, which reach lifted calls only as classical arguments, §4.2) MUST have a covering entry, else CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`. The private t and f are not values of the region for §6: the certified pair cleans them, the only cleanup that is not a §6.4 realisation. Under control the allocations are unconditional and every gate of both calls and of the exchange is controlled |

# xv4 — an exclusive proof verdict for a fixed classical image

Full revised proposal for council review, 2026-11-02; `b-isa-spec-xv4`.
Supersedes `2026-11-02-contract-xv4.md`; proposed contract text only.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §5.2 includes every interface-region cell value in D. §12.2
K69–K74 gives `proof` only for componentwise full D; successful exhaustive
strict slices receive `evidence`. Dialect 0.3 draft §§19.9/25.2 correctly
requests that existing evidence label. Assigning it is not a checker bug.

North Star §5.1 calls exhaustive verification on a declared finite domain
a proof, while §8 rung 4(a) fixes the SQLite database classically and
varies all rows and all target values. This is a certificate terminology
and scope mismatch, not missing SQLite execution semantics. The proposal
adds a conditional proof without asserting correctness on other images.
It retains all varying input, target, fault, dirty-system and inherited
control components; neither sampling nor source edits replace the rung.

## Alternatives and complete tiny witness

| Choice | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Keep K74 | Current conformance; document the terminology mismatch. |
| Call an image slice unqualified proof | Unsoundly promotes correctness to every database image. |
| Checked image-relative proof | Recommended; the image is an explicit precondition. |
| Put the database in a local image | Existing full-external-D proof for a different specialized closure and preparation route; does not relabel the original interface-region claim. |

Let an exact, fault-free oracle at a sufficient bound have one input bit
x, one target bit t, four fault bits f and one `in` region[1,8] db, with
payload `db[x] & 1`. Its complete tuple relation is
`(x,t,f,db) -> (x,t XOR (db[x]&1),f,db)`, workspace clean.

For image `0102`, x=0 flips t and x=1 preserves it: payload `1 XOR x`.
For `0201`, payload is x. Each image slice has 64 tuples; unrestricted D
has 2^22 tuples, so coverage is exactly 1/65536. An implementation
hardcoding `0102` passes the first 64 and fails all 64 for the swapped
image. Fresh bindings and replay can prove the second image separately;
its mere difference is not malformed input.

An ordinary one-bit X action with no regions has domain t=0,1 and table
0→1, 1→0. Its full-D exhaustive check must receive only `proof`, whether
fixed_regions is absent or empty. This witness catches the first note's
vacuously true image-relative condition.

At A=16,W=8, with x width 10, t width 1 and f width 4, there are 15
varying bits and 524288 fixed image bits. Coverage against unrestricted D
is exactly 1/2^524288, never floating-point zero. Prepared cells remain
interface components even though the experiment fixes them.

## Recommended exact spec changes

Extend the §12.2 domain schema and the certificate label-request/checker
outcome enumerations; add a §0.2 K entry. Replace BOTH the introductory
"otherwise evidence" rule in §12.2 and K74's blanket strict-slice
classification with the exclusive classifier below. This is a proposed
in-version certificate extension; no instruction or domain D changes.

```text
A domain MAY contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}]. An absent
field is an empty list. name resolves by the existing component rules to
an in-role interface region, with precisely the subject's A and W.
Each resolved fixed region occurs exactly once and MUST NOT also occur
in domain.regions. A mut-role region cannot be fixed by this mechanism.

image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) raw bytes encoded as lowercase hexadecimal;
unused high bits in the final byte MUST be zero. Cell i consists of bits
[iW,(i+1)W) in the little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of the raw
bytes, not the hexadecimal text. The checker hashes independently and
uses those cells on every replay. Fixed cells participate in domain
validity and tuple/output checks, but each contributes one choice to n.
The full unrestricted |D| remains unchanged. This metadata introduces no
operation for inspecting unknown coherent state.

After every §12.1 replay obligation succeeds, an exhaustive
table/program/oracle claim receives exactly one outcome, in this order:
(1) proof iff its domain equals unrestricted D component by component;
(2) otherwise proof_relative_to_image iff fixed_regions is nonempty,
    every image binding is valid, every fixed region is accounted for,
    and every remaining component occurs exactly once with its full
    range (all interface bundles/cells, borrowed positions and prepended
    inherited controls included);
(3) otherwise evidence.
Successful sampled claims always receive evidence, including when their
distinct draws cover the whole domain. Malformed domains, stale hashes
and mismatches reject, rather than receiving any successful verdict.
Omission-at-zero remains a strict slice unless the original component
is thereby genuinely at its full range under the existing rules.

Coverage remains exact n/|D| against unrestricted D, with distinct-draw
coverage for sampling. The checker report identifies closure_hash,
claims-array index, invocation/context key, and ordered resolved
(region identity,A,W,image hash) bindings. A supplied label is a request,
not a producer assertion. Requested proof rejects unless the computed
outcome is proof; requested proof_relative_to_image rejects unless the
computed outcome is proof_relative_to_image. An evidence request may
accompany a stronger independently computed outcome, as before.

K75's host-limit handling remains: unchecked (host limit) discharges no
proof obligation and does not invalidate a legal domain or shrink it.
```

This deliberately preserves full-D K74 proofs and evidence for ordinary
strict slices. The sole new successful classification is a nonempty,
valid image binding with all other components fully quantified.

```text
A proof_relative_to_image theorem is conditional on the stated image.
Using it to discharge an executable invocation additionally requires
checked preparation/binding establishing that this in-region contains
exactly those classical bytes and is factorised from other systems at
entry, and that its exit image is restored. Establish preparation from
declared bytes and logical preparation operations, never inspection of
unknown coherent data. Without that preparation evidence the theorem
remains conditional and does not discharge the invocation.

This verdict cannot discharge an unrestricted borrow-frame, inverse-pair
or other full-D obligation, and cannot be reused for another image,
invocation, target realisation or schedule by discarding its bindings.
All §12.1 exact +1 basis-phase, full-tuple, clean-workspace and
external-reference obligations remain in force. A basis table cannot
prove a non-permutation realisation; its numerical/channel obligations
are unchanged. Target matching, resource accounting and error
composition are not relaxed by conditional correctness.
```

For fixed image I, write J_I for the image-preparation isometry including
prescribed clean workspace. The permutation assertion is `V J_I = P J_I`,
also after tensoring with identity on an arbitrary external reference.
When a process claim is needed, compare
`E_real ∘ Ad_J_I` and `E_ref ∘ Ad_J_I` with the complete output interface.
Controlled-unitary comparison retains a phase-sensitive bound on
`||U J_I − V J_I||`; equal uncontrolled channels alone cannot discharge it.
Numerical/channel acceptance procedures remain those of §12.1.

## Mixed fat object (bennett-4ht)

Add to §§9/10/12: "A native binary may carry VM objects only for marked
modules. The host launch manifest selects a VM entry and records the
classical image preparation/copy into its in-region, with resolved shape
and raw-byte hash. The certificate inventory is that entry's selected VM
closure and reached contexts, not the whole native executable. Image
preparation may come from host database construction or a declared
snapshot. Host bytes are bound to device cells using the declared packing,
not host pointer identity. Unmarked main, argv/envp, libc and unrelated
code remain native. The native comparison invokes the corresponding
materialized marked entry over the same image and varying domain; it
need not treat the entire binary as the oracle. Runtime shims and build
configuration are recorded as required by the North Star."

A preparation witness for this note is a host buffer `01 02` copied to
the selected two-cell VM in-region before entry and retained unchanged
through the oracle's return. Preparing an entangled region does not meet
that classical-image precondition. This says how a mixed object uses the
contract; a physical launch shim need not be implemented by this note.

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

| Item | Change and concrete witness |
|---|---|
| sol XV4-B01: new rule overlaps full-D proof and K74 strict-slice evidence | Replace both blanket rules with one ordered, exclusive classifier; require nonempty fixed_regions. Full-D X receives proof, the 64-tuple 0102 slice receives proof_relative_to_image with 1/65536 coverage, sampling receives evidence. |

The additional round-1 suggestions are retained: a complete oracle
relation, stale-versus-fresh image mutations, explicit preparation-isometry
comparison, and separation of requested labels from checker outcomes.

## Hashes, publication and consumers

Fixed images and requested labels change certificate bytes; assigned
verdicts and resolved bindings are checker-report data. Under an
authorized 0.9 K extension retaining the semantic envelope, §9.2 excludes
certificate sections from object_hash, so source/interface/closure/object
identities, targets, schedules and ref catalog remain unchanged. A full
version bump instead requires regenerating version-envelope identities
and dependent pins. This note authorizes neither publication choice.

Producers/checkers must publish schema and verdict vectors together;
legacy schemas must not silently treat the qualified spelling as proof.
Until acceptance retain current evidence labels and exact fractions.
The SQLite rung still requires unmodified SQLite, real SQL, quantum
addressing, all rows/targets at N≤2^10, Grover at N≤2^8 and the named-target
N=2^20 estimate. Conditional correctness does not replace those gates.
xv4 remains open for acceptance and implementation.

## Conformance vectors and verification

In addition to the three exclusive-verdict cases: swapping 0102 to 0201
while retaining the old digest, expected relation or preparation binding
rejects reuse. Updating all bindings permits a fresh check, never reuse
of the old theorem. Test hashing hex text instead of bytes, wrong A/W,
nonzero padding, duplicate region identities, omitted target/fault/dirty
ranges, partial controls, a requested unqualified proof and a mut-region
binding. Identical image strings for two distinct resolved region slots
remain separate bindings. Test outer controls 0/1, arbitrary dirty states
and external references; sampled full coverage remains evidence.

This exact finite witness checks hashes, the complete 64-tuples relations,
exclusive classification premises and coverage. It is not execution of a
new checker/schema, a SQLite run or council acceptance.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
import hashlib
import itertools

images = (bytes([1, 2]), bytes([2, 1]))
hashes = [hashlib.sha256(i).hexdigest() for i in images]
assert hashes == [
    'a12871fee210fb8619291eaea194581cbd2531e4b23759d225f6806923f63222',
    '25dfd29c09617dcc9852281c030e5b3037a338a4712a42a21c907f259c6412a0']
assert hashes[0] != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
rows = list(itertools.product(range(2), range(2), range(16)))
assert len(rows) == 64 and F(len(rows), 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
for image in images:
    for x, t, f in rows:
        payload = image[x] & 1
        after = (x, t ^ payload, f, image)
        assert (after[0], after[1] ^ payload, after[2], after[3]) == (x, t, f, image)
assert sum((images[0][x] & 1) != (images[1][x] & 1) for x, t, f in rows) == 64
assert all((images[0][x] & 1) == (1 ^ x) for x in range(2))
assert all((images[1][x] & 1) == x for x in range(2))
# Full-D X: empty image set cannot satisfy the qualified predicate.
full, fixed, remaining_full = True, [], True
assert full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
# Fixed-image slice: not full D, but every other component is full.
full, fixed, remaining_full = False, [hashes[0]], True
assert not full and bool(fixed) and remaining_full
# Omit a target range: neither proof predicate holds.
remaining_full = False
assert not full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
assert 2**16 * 8 == 524288
assert F(2**15, 2**(15 + 524288)) == F(1, 2**524288)
print('PASS xv4: two 64-tuple image relations; 64 swapped-image failures; exclusive proof predicates; exact coverage')
```
# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh

from pathlib import Path

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

# Owned deliverable structure and local links.
base = Path('docs/notes')
ids = ['z4c', 'l2p', 'fd0', 'lpe', 'adg', 'xv4', 'b5b', '5u3',
       'checker-native-model', 'checker-diagnostics', 'checker-fault-rate',
       'checker-resource-contexts']
for ident in ids:
    path = base / ('2026-11-02-contract-' + ident + '.md')
    body = path.read_text()
    for word in ['Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                 'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes',
                 'Conformance', 'Release']:
        assert word.lower() in body.lower(), (ident, word)
    assert not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', body)
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in body.splitlines()) % 2 == 0
    for target in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', body):
        if not target.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#')):
            assert (path.parent / target.split('#')[0]).exists(), (ident, target)
print('PASS: 12 proposals; exact AND 8 columns/32 inverse columns; '
      '128 corrected branch columns; 3 negative channels; '
      'model/domain/orbit/resource arithmetic; fences and local links')
print('Fixed-image SHA-256:', image_hash)
```

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## 5. Regions

### 5.1 Interfaces and common well-formedness

Signatures list roles: `oracle @o (in a: wire[8]) (tgt t: wire[8]) (fault
f: wire[4]) (mem db: region[10,8] in) (args n: i64)`. **Interface
regions** are caller-owned, passed by name, role `in` (returned to entry
contents on every basis configuration) or `mut` (actions only). In the
text form (§16.2) a wire param's role suffix is optional and, when
present, MUST equal its group's role (`in` in an oracle's or action's
`in` group, `mut` in an action's `mut` group); a region param always
carries its role (`in` or `mut`); other region-interface params carry
no suffix. Lproc parameter roles are specified in §3.7 and §16.2. A region
MUST NOT contain `measure effect br call uncall trap hostcall qreg.* budget.mint` or
classical memory instructions, nor lifted rows outside oracle bodies.
Predicate stack empty at entry and exit. Every `qalloc` matched by a
`qfree` of a clean bundle, every `qborrow` by a `qreturn` after its
frame, every local `qregion` restored, by **explicit cleanup
instructions**; the schedule chooses only realisations. **`in` wires in
actions** appear only as controls (including the address operands of
`qload qunload qstore qexchm`, the wires pushed by `qpred`, the selector
of `qif`, whose two `x` gates restore it, and the `cond` wires of `qloop …
while`), as targets of diagonal gates, as `qcopy`/`qxor` sources, as the
value operand of `qstore` (it is only read, as the control of the XOR
into the selected cell), or as `in` arguments of calls. The **region
call graph** — an edge from a function to the callee of each of its
`oracle_call`, `action_call`, `lift.oracle_call`, `lift.oracle_uncall`
and `lift.perm_call` instructions and to both callees of each
`qinplace`, an oracle counting the rows of its lproc closure as its own —
is acyclic (suite F); recursion exists only through `lift.call` among
lprocs.

### 5.2 Lifted oracle

**Body**: lifted and control rows over its locations and lproc closure;
**exact-permutation only** (ε = 0 everywhere, transitively).

**Contract** on every x ∈ D: `ins` unchanged; `tgt` final = initial ⊕
payload(F_B(x)); `fr` final = initial ⊕ code(F_B(x)); `in` regions
unchanged; borrowed bundles returned (§5.6); workspace, locations, local
regions, step state clean / at image. **F_B(x)** = `(f(x), 0)` if the
reference interpreter reaches `lift.retval` within B steps without a
fault (f(x) = the XOR-accumulated `result_val`), else `(0, code)` with
`code` the first fault in sequential order, or `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` if
not halted after B steps. The oracle is an **involution** on `(ins, tgt,
fr)` and its own adjoint.

**Construction**: (1) allocate step state (`result_val` included),
locations, local regions, `alloctab` pre-entries, B frames; (2) run B
forward steps; (3) **commit**: `at_HALT = (pc == HALT)`; under `at_HALT ∧
¬faulted`: `qxor result_val tgt`; under `faulted`: `qxor code fr`; under
`¬at_HALT`: XOR the constant `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` into `fr`; uncompute
`at_HALT`; (4) **prefix reversal**: run the inverse step for t = B..1,
then release (1). The commit writes persist.

**Domain D**: every value of each `ins`, of `tgt`, of `fr`, of every cell
of every `in` region, of every borrowed bundle; `qalloc`ed wires at 0;
local regions at image.

### 5.3 Quantum action

**Semantics.** The body denotes a unitary U on mut ⊗ ins ⊗ mem ⊗
(borrowed), where mem includes both `mut` and `in` interface regions,
block-diagonal in the computational basis of `ins` and of `in`-region
cells, identity on borrowed and external reference systems (§5.6),
workspace returned clean, with domain D every value of each `mut`
and `in` bundle, every cell of every `mut` and `in` interface region and
every borrowed bundle, workspace initially at 0 and local regions at
image. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-5.2-P4, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-5.6-P1, ISA-14.2] No fault outcome. Coherent internal
control yields the padded unitary under declared bounds, part of the
action's identity. **Permutation action**: realised unitary a
permutation matrix (ε = 0, permutation gates only, transitively).

**Body**: gates; `qcopy quncopy qxor qexch qinplace`; `qload qunload
qstore qexchm`; `qpred qunpred qif qloop`; `oracle_call action_call`;
`qalloc qborrow qfree qreturn`; `qregion`.
An action body with several `block`s executes its blocks in text order,
each a unitary fragment; labels are descriptive and carry no control
semantics, and lifetimes may span blocks.

**Bounds.** The `bounds { … }` list of an action's `regs` line and the
`bounds` array of its signature hold, in text order, the trip count `n`
of every `qloop` and `qloop … while` in the body (nested loops
included, outer before inner); the list MUST equal the body (suite F)
and is empty for a body without loops. It is part of the action's
identity because the padded unitary of coherent internal control
depends on it.

**Adjoint table** (reverse order, instruction replaced, versions
renumbered):

```
  rx/ry/rz(θ) ↦ (−θ)   s ↔ sdg   t ↔ tdg   x cx ccx mcx swap h cz: self-adjoint
  qalloc ↔ qfree   qborrow ↔ qreturn   qregion creation ↔ release   qcopy ↔ quncopy   qload ↔ qunload
  qxor qexch qstore qexchm: self-adjoint   qinplace @g @ginv tk x (ins) (mem) (args) ↦ qinplace @ginv @g tk x (ins) (mem) (args)
  oracle_call ↦ oracle_call
  action_call @b ↦ adj action_call @b   qpred/qunpred pairs reversed as a whole
  qif w {A}{B} ↦ adjoint of the complete §4.6 expansion, including unconditional lifetimes, in reverse order. [deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7]
  qloop n {A} ↦ qloop n {adj A}
  qloop n while cond {A} ↦ flags computed forward as §4.6; adj A for i = n..1 under c_i; flags uncomputed
```

**Control.** `ctl w @a` is controlled-U: workspace, regions and flags
allocated unconditionally; every gate of the body and its cleanup runs
under `w` in addition to the active predicate.

The shorthand `qif w {adj A}{adj B}` denotes that adjoint when each
arm's allocations and releases are matched within that arm; no
arm-local lifetime restriction is imposed on the source `qif`.
[deps: ISA-3.5, ISA-4.6-P7, ISA-5.1]

Two operations form a **matched pair** iff the second is the §5.3
adjoint of the first, with the same callee(s) (the pair exchanged for
`qinplace`), identical classical argument values and token identities
and tuples, the same operand wires with the first's read-only input
versions and produced target versions current at the second, and equal
effective control sets including the versions of their wires; order and
repetition in predicate stacks are irrelevant. [deps: ISA-2-P10, ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-5.4]
A matched inverse restores preceding wire-version bindings for
subsequent matching only when the §6.2 cleanup conditions hold; matching
alone does not discharge an outstanding-reader or phase obligation, and
a cell write always advances its cell version even when its value is
restored. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-6.1-P1, ISA-6.2]

Scheduled action adjoints use the source replacements above before
reference-recipe instantiation, with the ordinal contract and `adj`
adapter specified in §11.1 (K62). [deps: ISA-5.3-P4, ISA-11-P2, ISA-11.1-P29]

### 5.4 Budgets and fault codes

# xv4 — proof relative to a fixed classical region image

Proposal for council review; `b-isa-spec-xv4`.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

ISA 0.9 §5.2's D includes every value of every interface-region cell;
§12.2 K69–K74 assigns `proof` only for componentwise full D. A successful
exhaustive slice is `evidence`, even if every varying component of the
actual experiment was enumerated. Dialect 0.3 draft §§19.9/25.2 correctly
requests today's evidence label. The existing checker is not defective
for assigning it.

North Star §5.1 defines proof as exhaustive over a declared finite domain,
and §8 rung 4(a) explicitly fixes the database classically before entry
while varying all rows and all target values. There is a **certificate
scope/terminology mismatch**, not missing SQLite execution semantics.
Keeping an explicitly conditional proof restores that meaning without
claiming correctness for other databases. Shrinking the varying input,
fault, target, dirty, or inherited-control domain is not an acceptable
substitute; neither are SQLite source edits or a sampled row check.
The exact underlying machine capability is already present. Under the
rubric, judge the gap against the promised proof meaning, not as a
failure of existing K74 replay or of the rung-4 evidence gate.

## Alternatives and worked example

For a tiny oracle with one row-index bit, one target bit, four fault bits,
and one `in` region[1,8], the unrestricted D has 22 bits. Fix image bytes
`01 02`; enumerate all x,t,f values: 64 cases. That is fraction
`2^6/2^22 = 1/65536` of full D, so current K74 says evidence. All 64
cases nonetheless prove the oracle relation for this image if the
realisation is exact phase-free permutation code with full tuple checks.
An implementation that always substitutes bytes `01 02` agrees on this
slice and fails on `02 01`; the conditional proof must not certify it
for the latter image.

At the supplied rung sizes A=16,W=8, x has 10 bits, t one bit and f
four bits: 15 varying bits and 524288 fixed region bits. The fraction
is exactly `1/2^524288` when no further components are present. Do not
round it to zero. Prepared image bytes are a precondition, not absent
interface components.

| Alternative | Consequence |
|---|---|
| Keep K74 unchanged | Valid current conformance, but a finite exhaustive fixed-image experiment keeps the evidence label; document the North Star terminology gap explicitly. |
| Call the slice an unqualified proof | Unsound promotion to all database images. |
| Conditional proof with checked binding (recommended) | Proves exactly the prepared-image relation and keeps unconditional K74 proof distinct. |
| Embed the database as a local region image | Can prove that specialized closure on its full external D; changes identity and preparation route, and does not relabel the original interface-region claim. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Extend §12.2 domain records and add a §0.2 K entry:

```text
A domain MAY additionally contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}].
name resolves to an in-role interface region by the existing component
rules. image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) bytes as lowercase hexadecimal;
unused high bits of the final byte, if any, MUST be zero. Cell i is bits
[iW,(i+1)W) of that little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of these
raw bytes, not their hexadecimal spelling. A fixed region must occur
exactly once, cannot also occur in domain.regions, and must have the
subject's exact A and W. The checker independently hashes the image and
uses its cells on every replay. Mut-role regions cannot use this binding.

An exhaustive table/program/oracle claim that succeeds on every element,
has every non-fixed component at its full range, and binds all remaining
components only by fixed_regions receives proof_relative_to_image.
The checker reports the region closure hash, claim index, invocation or
context key, and ordered (region identity,A,W,image hash) bindings with
that verdict. It also reports coverage against the original unrestricted
D. This is a proof only on the stated fixed-image subspace. Sampled
claims always remain evidence, including on that subspace. Unqualified
proof retains K74's full-D condition.

Use of proof_relative_to_image for an executable invocation additionally
requires a checked preparation/binding establishing that the in-region
has exactly that classical image and is factorised from other systems
at entry, and that the region restores it at exit. Preparation is checked
from the declared classical bytes and logical preparation operations,
not by inspecting unknown coherent data. Without this precondition the
claim remains a conditional theorem and cannot discharge that invocation.
This verdict cannot discharge a full-D borrow-frame, inverse-pair or
other unrestricted obligation, nor be silently reused for another image.
All phase, full-tuple, external-reference and invocation-binding
obligations of §12.1 continue to apply.
```

Extend the checker verdict enumeration to include the spelling above;
requested `proof_relative_to_image` must be checked just like K74's
requested labels. Non-permutation lowerings retain process/channel
obligations; this is not permission to certify them from a basis table.
Where such a conditional process claim is needed, explicitly compare
the channel after the same image-preparation isometry on both sides.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

No source closure, interface, target or catalog bytes change. The
certificate's fixed image and conditional verdict alter its own bytes;
an image change invalidates the binding even if the region hash is the
same. Plugin, checker and rung gates must retain the qualified verdict
and hash visibly. Schemas must not silently accept it as old `proof`.
Until acceptance consumers must keep current evidence labels and exact
fractions; the new spelling is only proposed here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Use the 64-case tiny example; its raw-byte hash is computed in
[contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md). Change one
image byte, swap cells, change W, hash the hex text, omit a target/fault
range, or request unqualified proof: reject that requested promotion.
Sampling all 64 cases remains evidence. Two identical image strings at
different resolved region identities are separate bindings. Test outer
control 0/1 and dirty systems in full; a preparation entangled with a
reference does not meet the fixed-image premise. These are proposal
vectors; neither a rung-4 gate nor a new checker test was run here.

## Release and after acceptance

Recommend a **0.9 K certificate clarification/extension** if the council
accepts a new qualified outcome; preserve every old K74 label. Add it to
schema and checker vectors together before revising rung acceptance.
The broader North Star rung remains unmodified SQLite, real SQL,
quantum-addressed memory, all rows/targets at N≤2^10, Grover at N≤2^8,
and the N=2^20 resource estimate. xv4 stays open for that decision.

## 12. Certificate (cert 0.9; JCS)

### 12.1 Claims

| kind | payload | checker obligation |
|---|---|---|
| `exhaustive` | `domain` (§12.2); `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }` \| `"inverse_pair"` | re-execute every domain element on the reference interpreter / expected program; compare full tuples. For `"inverse_pair"` (the claim of an `inverse_pairs` entry, §12.3): for every element (x, ins, mem cells, args) run g and ginv at the entry's tuple, token-typed arguments at their `arg_tokens` tuples, and require both fault-free, ginv(g(x)) = x and g(ginv(x)) = x; the bundles and regions components MUST be the full domain of g's `in` and `mem` slots (the `args` component lists the covered classical values) |
| `sampled` | `domain`; `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }`; `subject` and invocation binding as below (K13); `sampler`: `{ "algorithm": "xoshiro256**", "seed": [4 decimal strings, not all zero], "count": n }` | independently replay both sides of the declared relation on every sampled element; compare full tuples; always **evidence**, even if every element is drawn |
| `structural` | `{ "rule": "translation_lemma" \| "inverse_pair_library" \| "adjoint_table", "subclaims": [indices], "witness": … }`; for `translation_lemma`: `{ "V": [points], "W": [points], "X": [wires], "B": [wires], "frame": [first point, last point] }`; for `inverse_pair_library`: `{ "library_closure_hash", "claim_index" }`; for `adjoint_table`: `{}` | check the rule's side conditions syntactically against the object and the subclaims' results |
| `numerical` | `{ "subject": { "closure_hash", "variant", "inherited_controls", "schedule_hash" }, "metric": "operator_norm", "precision_bits": n, "bound": decimal }` | **checker-computed, for every invocation context of the subject** (below): build the realised unitary U of that invocation (the region's realisation under the root schedule at that path, with its resolved classical arguments, budget, epsilon shares and cleanup choices, and `inherited_controls` control wires prepended) and the reference unitary V (the region's §5.3 semantics with the same arguments, controlled on the same wires) at the subject's signature widths in interval arithmetic at `precision_bits` (basis order = allocation order, controls first), form interval enclosures of `V†U − I` entrywise, derive the operator-norm bound as the Frobenius-norm upper bound in interval arithmetic, and verify it is ≤ `bound`; the claim holds iff it holds for every context |
| `channel` | `{ "subject": as above, "metric": "diamond", "precision_bits", "bound", "dual": [ { "Y": interval Hermitian matrix, "mu": decimal } … ] }` | for every invocation context of the subject, in enumeration order, verify its dual witness for the **diamond-norm SDP** below in interval arithmetic and `mu` ≤ `bound` (one witness per context; too few witnesses: reject) |

**CLAIM-REPLAY** (K13; b-isa-spec-9uy, from bennett-vm-clw).
Sampled observations remain evidence under the following replay contract.

**Comparison relation.** `exhaustive` and `sampled` claims
MUST name their `subject: { closure_hash, variant, inherited_controls,
schedule_hash? }` and an explicit `expected`; there is no implicit
self-comparison or default expected relation. Without a schedule the
left side is the subject's reference semantics at the bound invocation;
with one it is that invocation's realised operation. `expected: "oracle"`
compares the realised operation to §5.2's reference oracle map and
requires `schedule_hash`. [deps: ISA-5.2, ISA-11, ISA-12.1-P11] `expected: {program: H}` independently runs
the reference semantics of linked closure H at the **same** arguments,
budget and interface tuple; its signature MUST be compatible.
`expected: {table: rows}` compares to the explicit full-tuple rows
below. The checker MUST compare every interface output, preserve inputs
and borrowed systems, and verify clean workspace and exact +1 basis
phase for a permutation assertion (T3); matching a target or fault
projection alone, or a producer's claimed verdict, is insufficient. [deps: ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-5.6]
For a non-permutation realisation, use the existing numerical/channel
claims rather than labeling basis observations a permutation proof. [deps: ISA-12.1-P4, ISA-12.1-P5, ISA-14.2]
K4's `expected: "inverse_pair"` retains its separate two-composition
relation and entry/domain bindings; it is not a sampled proof option.

**Table tuples.** Each table row is `{ before: tuple,
after: tuple }`. A tuple is `{ bundles: ["<unsigned integer>", …],
borrowed: ["<unsigned integer>", …], regions: [["<cell>", …], …] }`.
`bundles` lists inherited control wires first (each width 1), then all
signature bundles in operand order, **including arbitrary initial
oracle target and fault bundles**. `borrowed` lists dirty bundles in
allocation order, with widths fixed by the checked borrow lifetimes;
`regions` lists interface regions in signature order, every cell in
increasing address order, each as an unsigned W-bit integer. All
integers use canonical decimal strings, within the declared widths;
no cells, inputs or unchanged components may be omitted. Workspace is
not an interface component and MUST finish zero. Rows are in domain
enumeration order, one per distinct declared domain element (including
for `sampled`); duplicate/missing/out-of-range rows reject. This favors
an explicit table; `{program: H}` provides the compact relation when a
full table is large. Sampling draws with replacement using the existing
sampler; coverage counts distinct drawn elements, not draws. A mismatch
rejects; success is always `evidence` with that distinct covered fraction
of D, including when the draws happen to cover all of D.

**CLAIM-REPLAY witness**

```json
{
  "invocation":{"args":["2"],"budget":["2","0","0","0"]},
  "row":{
    "before":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
    "after":{"bundles":["1","1","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]}
  },
  "wrong_row":{
    "before":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
    "after":{"bundles":["1","2","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]}
  },
  "changed_arg_after":{"bundles":["1","0","3"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
  "short_budget_after":{"bundles":["1","2","2"],"borrowed":["1"],"regions":[["2","1"]]},
  "sampler":{"algorithm":"xoshiro256**","seed":["1","0","0","0"],"count":4},
  "widths":{"bundles":[2,2,4],"borrowed":[1],"regions":[[8,8]]},
  "sample_count":4,"distinct_samples":1,"covered_fraction":"1/33554432","verdict":"evidence"
}
```

The subject is a two-row oracle body `r = lift.add x a;`
`lift.retval r`, widths x = target = a = 2 and fault = 4, with a preserved
one-bit dirty bundle and an unused two-cell, eight-bit `in` interface
region; no inherited controls. [deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8] A legal layout has ptr_width=2, H=2, A_tab=1, S_cells=0,
null_size=1, no globals, the interface mapped at base 1 with extent 2,
heap_base=3 and heap_limit_max=0; the token is (2,0,0,0), and the unused
mapping is retained even though the body has no load/store. [deps: ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-5.4]
The **body** does not access memory;
the interface cells remain part of D. [deps: ISA-7.2, ISA-12.2] The reference payload is
(1 + 2) mod 4 = 3: target 2 XOR 3 = 1 and fault 3 XOR 0 = 3.
The singleton declared domain is precisely `row.before`, within the
full 25-bit D (2+2+4+1+16 bits). [deps: ISA-5.4, ISA-7.2, ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.2] Four samples of singleton components draw no generator
words and cover one of 33554432 full tuples. [deps: ISA-12.1-P8, ISA-12.1-P13, ISA-12.2] An identity implementation
would agree with itself but MUST fail against `row`; using `wrong_row`
also MUST fail. At argument 1 the target is 2 XOR 2 = 0; at B = 1 the
payload is zero and fault is 3 XOR 1 = 2. A certificate for argument 2,
B = 2 cannot be replayed as either of those invocations. The printed
rows illustrate the relation; bound closure/object hashes still have
to be supplied in an actual certificate, not invented for this witness.

**Invocation contexts.** A subject denotes the set of invocations of
the region `closure_hash`, with the given `variant` and `inherited_
controls`, in the root schedule `schedule_hash`: every invocation of
the region in the root realisation's complete invocation tree —
including invocations that no schedule entry names and defaults alone
cover (§11) — with the classical arguments, budget, `epsilon_share`s and
cleanup realisations resolved for it. An invocation's **context key** is
its concrete invocation chain (§11), outermost first, each element `{
"symbol", "point", "iteration", "instance", "call_index" }` with every
iteration index, fixed-binding key and forward semantic-call occurrence
resolved
and, for an element inside an oracle, `"step"` (1..B) and `"phase"`
(`"fwd"` or `"rev"`) added; contexts are enumerated in increasing
bytewise order of the JCS encodings of their keys (wildcard selectors
play no part). [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-11, ISA-11.1-P9]
Every claim bound and every
`error` entry (§12.3) is a **common upper bound over all contexts** of
its key; the multiplicity composition of §12.3 is sound for that reason.

**Diamond-norm SDP** (Watrous, *Theory of Computing* 5 (2009), §4; the
Hermiticity-preserving simplification). Let Φ = Φ_real − Φ_ref be the
difference of the subject's realised channel and the reference channel
(both on the subject's interface, controls included), d_in = d_out = d
the dimension. The **Choi matrix** is J(Φ) = Σ_{i,j} |i⟩⟨j| ⊗ Φ(|i⟩⟨j|)
(input factor first, output factor second, computational basis in
allocation order, **unnormalised**). The witness is a Hermitian Y of
dimension d² and a decimal mu. The checker accepts iff, with every
matrix entry an interval and every test rigorous (an inconclusive test
rejects): Y − J(Φ) ⪰ 0, Y + J(Φ) ⪰ 0, mu·I − Tr_out(Y) ⪰ 0 (partial trace
over the output factor), and mu ≤ `bound`; then ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu (the dual of
Watrous's SDP with Y₀ = Y₁ = Y; this is the diamond norm itself, not
half of it). *(non-normative)* With this input-first Choi convention
Tr_out traces the second factor, Tr[(ρ ⊗ I)Y] = Tr[ρ Tr_out(Y)]; a
primal formulation that labels the input factor ρᵀ ranges over the same
set of density matrices, so the bound is unchanged. Encoding: `Y` is a row-major array of entries `[re_lo, re_
hi, im_lo, im_hi]` (decimal strings). **Positivity test.** The checker
proves each of the three matrices **positive definite** by an interval
Cholesky (or Hermitian LDL†) factorisation in which the interval lower
bound of every pivot is strictly positive, and rejects otherwise; no
other test is admitted (nonnegative leading principal minors do not
imply positive semidefiniteness: diag(0, −1)). Producers make witnesses
strictly feasible by replacing Y with Y + δI and mu with mu′ = mu + δ·(d
+ 1) for a small rational δ > 0 (any mu′ > mu + δ·d suffices): then Y ±
J(Φ) ⪰ δI ≻ 0 and, since Tr_out(Y + δI) = Tr_out(Y) + δ·d·I, mu′·I −
Tr_out(Y + δI) ⪰ δI ≻ 0; ‖Φ‖◇ ≤ mu′, which MUST be ≤ `bound`. That
recipe assumes an exactly feasible witness. For a numerically obtained
witness the producer first certifies lower bounds −η₊, −η₋, −η_s on the
smallest eigenvalues of Y − J(Φ), Y + J(Φ) and mu·I − Tr_out(Y), chooses
δ > max(η₊, η₋, η_s) and publishes Y′ = Y + δI and mu′ = mu + (d + 1)·δ:
then Y′ ± J(Φ) ⪰ (δ − η±)I ≻ 0 and mu′·I − Tr_out(Y′) ⪰ (δ − η_s)I ≻ 0.
These margins make the matrices positive definite; they do not make the
interval test conclusive: every pivot enclosure the checker computes at
`precision_bits` MUST still have a strictly positive lower endpoint,
else the checker rejects (no fixed allowance such as 2^−`precision_bits`
bounds the interval error, which depends on d and conditioning). **Interval
arithmetic** at `precision_bits`
means every operation rounds outward to `precision_bits`-bit binary
rationals; transcendental entries (`rx ry rz`) are enclosed by a Taylor
series with the Lagrange remainder bound (§17 item 8 fixes the term
count). A `numerical` or `channel` subject's `schedule_hash` is required:
it names the realisation the claim is about. K13 also requires it for
`expected: "oracle"`; standalone reference relations use the explicit
invocation binding instead.

**Sampling.** For each domain component with range size R = hi − lo + 1,
assemble m = ⌈⌈log₂ R⌉/64⌉ consecutive generator words (least-significant
word first) into an integer, reduce modulo 2^⌈log₂ R⌉, reject and redraw
if ≥ R, add `lo`; components are consumed in enumeration order.

**Invocation identity witnesses (K63).** For `qinplace @G @G tk x`
at action A's `entry:0`, the two call edges below are distinct even with
identical arguments, and each prefix is also a round-trippable schedule
path selecting that invocation's descendants. [deps: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11]
For two `lift.call` sites forwarding root token positions 0 and 1 into
lproc f, whose `entry:0` contains one `lift.oracle_call`, the next two
keys distinguish the row instances at outer step 1, even if token values
happen to agree. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-12.1-P11]
The `instance`/`call_index` fields are present in both schedule path
prefixes and context keys; context-only step/phase fields below are
supplied separately as §11 selectors in the schedule form. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P5, ISA-12.1-P11]

```json
{
  "qinplace_context_keys": [
    [
      {
        "symbol": "aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa",
        "point": "entry:0",
        "iteration": [],
