../bennett/docs/council/contract-xv4-2026-11-02/round-1/raw-sol-1.txt:4796:../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md:148:| Quantum operations: allocate (clean / dirty / class), release, gate, rotate(θ), measure, quantum-addressed load and store | **Declared external marker functions** `__bennett_*` with fixed signatures, exactly as Enzyme's `__enzyme_autodiff`. Not `llvm.*` intrinsics (a plugin cannot register them). The plugin recognises them by name and signature at link time. **Positive legality requirement:** a marker is declared as an external function that may read and write memory and may not return (no `readnone`, `readonly`, `speculatable`, `willreturn`, `nounwind`-only combinations that would license elimination, commoning or speculation). Stock passes then preserve the *dynamic sequence* of marker calls on every executed path. | Containing functions may be inlined, cloned, unrolled or versioned, so the *static* count of marker call sites is not preserved; the external declaration itself is never inlined. *Explicit quantum registers* are accessed only through markers, never through LLVM `load`/`store`, so no alias-analysis or forwarding pass can touch them. The ordinary loads and stores of lifted classical code (all of SQLite's) are not quantum-register accesses: they are lifted coherently according to the incoming memory model, as classical memory whose contents happen to be superposed. This legality is a property of ordinary effectful external calls, not of any attribute we invent; the legality corpus (§3.2) tests it. |
../bennett/docs/council/contract-xv4-2026-11-02/round-1/raw-sol-1.txt:4798:../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md:153:| Control as structure: adjoint, controlled-by-qubit, compute-with-uncompute, reversible-tier entry | **Function granularity**: a region is outlined to a function (LLVM's `CodeExtractor`) and entered through a marker call `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, budget, args…)`, `__bennett_adj(@f, …)`, `__bennett_compute(@f, …)`, or `__bennett_reversible(@f, budget, …)` for the reversible tier. The plugin generates controlled and adjoint bodies on demand from one source body. **Well-formedness:** a `ctrl`/`adj`/`compute` region is unitary: user measurement inside it is refused (or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states); allocations inside are released clean or borrowed and restored; the compiler's own measurement-based uncomputation is the sole exception, and until its controlled form is fixed in the language's certificate kinds (§9.6) **unitary uncomputation is the fallback under control**. Exceptions and unwinding (`invoke`/`landingpad`, Rust panics, C++ `throw`) inside a region are step-machine control flow where the handler is in the region, and a fault code where it is not. | Front ends that want `when`-style syntax do the outlining themselves (Julia macro, clang attribute, Rust proc-macro) and emit the marker call; the dialect does not require it. Generating a body needs the callee's IR, which is why the plugin runs at link time (§3.3). |

## 1. Scope and conformance

### 1.1 What the ISA is

A program model for classical, reversible and coherent computation with
one semantics, executed by the VM (`bennett-vm`) and the circuit
specialiser (`bennett-circuits`), produced by the plugin
(`bennett-plugin`) through the dialect's reference semantics, scheduled
through §11, described for hardware by target descriptions (§8). Nothing
here refers to LLVM.

### 1.2 Conformance classes

| Class | Who | Obligation |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | VM, specialiser, hardware stacks | §3–§7, §14 exactly, and the checking of inverse-pair claims (§4.5, §12.1; a certificate applies when its `closure_hash` binding equals the region's); suites A–E, H |
| Producer | plugin, library compilers | well-formed objects (§9, §16) and regions (§5, §6); suite F |
| Scheduler | `bennett-sched` and competitors | §11; only schedules valid under §6; suite H |
| Checker | `bennett-conformance` | validates certificates (§12) without trusting producers |
| Target author | vendors | target descriptions (§8); suite E |

### 1.3 Deferred *(asserted non-semantic)*

Binary encodings (§16 text is the interchange form); mechanisation of
§14; device models beyond §7.3; the gate-name registry process; the
memory layout of the opaque C objects of §10–§11 (every constructor,
accessor and destructor is listed). **Minimality claims** (north star
§5) are not supported by cert 0.9 and MUST NOT appear in a certificate.
Decisions deferred to implementation, each with its default, are listed
in §17.

## 2. Terminology
### 3.3 Types, ownership, operand schema

```
  iN (1 ≤ N ≤ 128)   ptr (width ptr_width)   f64   token   qreg[n]   qreg[*]   region[A,W]
  wire   wire[N]      linear values in structured code and interfaces
  reg[N]              a location (oracle bodies, lprocs); not linear
```

**Widths.** The bound 1 ≤ N ≤ 128 applies to the type `iN` only (a lifted
row's `.iN` suffix names a location width, §4.2). `wire[N]`,
`reg[N]` and `qreg[n]` have N, n ≥ 1 and `region[A,W]` has A ≥ 0, W ≥ 1,
with no upper bound (a `qreg[*]` register's width is a run-time value n
≥ 0, §3.3 below); a value of these types is a bit-vector of its
width in every semantics (§14.1), including interface bundles,
pseudo-locations, locations, cells, stack cells and frame slots.

**Linear SSA with implicit versioning** (structured code and CC region
calls): a `wire`/`wire[N]` value is produced once and consumed once;
mode `in` consumes and re-produces a successor of the same name;
`consume` consumes; `produce` creates; a name is not used after
`consume`. **Regions are threaded the same way.** **Indexed operand**
`w[i]` (literal i) borrows wire i for one instruction.

**Disjointness.** The wire operands of one instruction are pairwise
distinct; **every wire of the effective control context is distinct from
every target and from every consumed or produced operand** of an
instruction executed under it (so `qpred p; x p` is ill-formed); a
predicate wire is not modified between its push and its matching pop; a
`qif`/`qloop … while` selector is not modified inside its arms or body.
A region's interface bundles and regions are pairwise disjoint.
Distinctness is judged on the wires denoted: a bundle and an indexed
operand of it, or two indexed operands denoting the same wire, overlap
and are not distinct. [deps: ISA-3.3-P3, ISA-4.4]

**Per-opcode operand schema** (§4.8): every opcode has a tier set, a
fixed arity, operand kinds and a result type; results are written first
(`w = op a b`), modes are never written, and **result types that are not
determined by the operands are written as a suffix** (`r = const.i32 5`,
`r = zext.i64 a`, `r = trunc.i8 a`, `r = ptrtoint.i64 p`, `p = inttoptr
a`). Width rules: `binop`: `iN` operands of equal width; `icmp`: `iN` or
`ptr` operands of equal width and the same type (CC/R; lifted forms:
§4.2); `select` arms equal width; `zext`/`sext` strictly widen, `trunc`
strictly narrows; `load.iN`
and `lift.load.iN` require N ∈ {8, 16, …, 128}; `measure` of `qreg[n]`
with n ≤ 128 yields `in`, and `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) of a `qreg[*]`
yields `iN`; signed division truncates toward zero. The
literal c of `const.iN c` and `lift.const.iN c` satisfies −2^(N−1) ≤ c <
2^N and denotes c mod 2^N (both the signed and the unsigned spelling of
every value are accepted); any other literal is ill-formed (suite F).

**Lifted width rules** (suite F; a mismatch is ill-formed, never a
truncation or an extension). Every address operand (`a` of `lift.load`/
`lift.store`, `p` of `lift.boundscheck`/`lift.free`) and every address
result (`w` of `lift.addr`/`lift.alloc`) is `reg[ptr_width]`; the length
`n` of `lift.alloc`/`lift.boundscheck` is a location of any width or a
literal in [0, 2^ptr_width); `inrange` and every prospective sum are
computed at width max(widths of the operands involved) + 1 with no wrap.
The destination of `lift.label` and the operand of `qjumpr` have any
width N with 2^N > n (the index-space size). `lift.call` location
arguments have exactly the callee's parameter widths and its destination the callee's
result width; its token, classical and region lists match the callee's
corresponding groups in count, order and type (§3.7); `lift.ret v` has
the containing lproc's result width;
`lift.oracle_call`/`lift.oracle_uncall`/`lift.perm_call` operands have
exactly the callee signature widths, and `mem` operands name caller
regions whose `[A,W]` equal the callee's and whose role is `in` for an
`in` slot (an `in` region is never passed to a `mut` slot); `fault C` and
`lift.faultif p C` require the literal C < 2^F, where F is the fault
width of the containing oracle or, in an lproc, of every oracle whose
lproc closure contains it (checked at load and again at link; an lproc
in no oracle's closure is checked when linked into one). The equal-width and widening/narrowing constraints above apply to lifted rows on their location and pseudo-location bit-vectors as specified in §4.2; the CC/R iN and ptr operand-type restrictions do not retype lifted locations. The destinations of one `lift.parmov` are pairwise distinct.

**Quantum registers in CC and R.** A **register** is n device-resident
wires of one class with a nonzero 64-bit **id**, recorded in the
program's **register ledger** with its class, width and state:
**live**, **viewed** (one slice of it is live) or **consumed**.
`qreg[n]` is the type of a register whose width n is fixed statically;
`qreg[*]` the type of a register whose width is known only at run time
(an unsigned 64-bit n ≥ 0). A `qreg[n]` value may be used wherever a
`qreg[*]` is expected; a `qreg[*]` value bound to a `qreg[n]` parameter
or block parameter (`call`, `br`, `ret`, launch) or to a slot of width n
(region call, §4.7) is checked at that transfer: its width MUST equal
the required one. The `qreg.*` instructions execute in CC and R;
`measure` only in CC. An instruction whose precondition fails is a CC
trap / R fault (§4.9) with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` unless the table says
otherwise, and has no effect. An id's value is chosen by the executor
(the suites compare ids only for equality); an id is never reassigned to
another register while the program is loaded. **Handle transitions:**

| Instruction | Precondition | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `q = qreg.new cls n` | `n` a literal n ≥ 1 (q: `qreg[n]`) or an `i64` value read unsigned, 0 included (q: `qreg[*]`); the device can provide n wires of class `cls`, else code `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` | q live, clean, with a fresh id and wires disjoint from every live or viewed register. [deps: ISA-3.3-P7] |
| `qreg.free q` | q live, not a view, clean on every basis configuration (a verifying executor checks it; on failure q stays live and unchanged; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14) | q consumed |
| `q2 = qreg.slice q lo len` | q live, not a view; `lo` and `len` each a literal (`len` ≥ 1) or an `i64` value, both unsigned; lo + len ≤ n, computed without wrap (a trap even when literals make the failure evident) | q2 a view of wires [lo, lo+len) of q, of type `qreg[len]` for a literal len and `qreg[*]` otherwise; q viewed; q2 MUST be the operand of exactly one region call and of nothing else; after that call q2 is consumed and q is live again; a use of q while it is viewed traps (the static rule below covers the cases suite F rejects). Reversing that region call (§7.4) restores q viewed and q2 live; reversing the slice frame then restores q live |
| `q = qreg.concat q1 q2` | q1, q2 live, not views, distinct registers | q1, q2 consumed; q live, with a fresh id, over the wires of q1 then q2; `qreg[n1+n2]` if both operands are static, else `qreg[*]` |
| `w = qreg.width q` | q live, not a view | w: `i64`, the width; no state change |
| `id = qreg.id q` | q live, not a view | id: `i64`, the bits of q's id; no state change |
| `q = qreg.from_id id` | `id` is the id of a register of the program that is live, not a view, and not owned by another run that is not CLOSED (§10) | q: `qreg[*]`, an alias of that register; no ledger state change; the run now owns the register (§10), which reversal does not undo (ownership ends when the run is CLOSED) |
| region call (§4.7) with q as operand | q live (a view included); its width equals the slot's (§3.3 binding); the wire sets filling distinct wire/region slots are pairwise disjoint, and an explicit control is disjoint from targets and consumed/produced operands (read-only input overlap remains allowed), else CC trap / R fault FAULT_UB_TRAP before any effect, including any view consumption. [deps: ISA-3.3-P4, ISA-4.7, ISA-4.9] | q unchanged. [deps: ISA-4.7] |
| `r = measure q` / `r = measure.iN q` | q live, not a view; the form `measure.iN` (1 ≤ N ≤ 128) for a `qreg[*]`, whose width MUST be N (checked before measuring); for a `qreg[n]` either `measure` or `measure.iN` with N = n (checked statically) | q consumed, its wires released; r: `in` / `iN` |
| reversal of a `qreg.*` frame (§7.4, §10) | – | the ledger state before that frame (below). A `measure` frame is **never reversed**: a segment containing one cannot be uncalled (§7.4) and a run containing one is not reversible (§10) |

**Ledger frames and their reversal** (§7.4). A `qreg.new` frame records
the id, class and width; its reversal releases that register without a
test: every later frame touching it has been undone first (LIFO) and the
run owns it exclusively (§10), so under T4's exactness hypothesis it is
clean, and otherwise its residual, within the declared bounds of the
undone realisations (§12.3), is discarded with it.
A `qreg.free` frame records the id, class, width and whether its
cleanliness was verified; its reversal re-creates the register clean and
live with the same id, class and width (every alias sees it live
again). A `qreg.slice` frame records the parent id, lo and len, a
`qreg.concat` frame the two operand ids and the new id; their reversal
restores the exact prior states and wire order without moving, copying
or measuring a wire. `qreg.width`, `qreg.id` and `qreg.from_id` change
no ledger state and their frames are pure (§7.4); if an `uncall` later
removes the register an alias refers to, every use of the alias traps.

**Binding**: a register of width n (`qreg[n]`, or `qreg[*]` of run-time
width n) fills a `wire[n]` slot bit for bit (bit 0 ↔ wire 0); it fills a
`region[A,W]` slot iff n = 2^A·W with cell i = bits [iW, (i+1)W); for a
`qreg[*]` the width is checked at the call, before any effect.
Host-supplied register ids are `qreg` values of the parameter's type.

The register ids supplied as arguments of one `bennett_run` MUST
denote pairwise disjoint registers, else `bennett_run` returns `E_ARG`
before executing the entry function or acquiring any register ownership.
[deps: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-10]

**Registers, handles and aliases.** A `qreg` value is a reference to a
register of the device; the state live / viewed / consumed belongs to
the register, not to a name. `qreg` values pass unchanged as `call`
arguments, `ret` values and `br` block arguments (each transfer binds
another name to the same register and changes no state; a `qreg[*]`
reaching a `qreg[n]` parameter is width-checked, above). Two names bound
to one register (by such transfers or by `qreg.from_id`) are
**aliases**: a transition
made through either is observed through both. The wire operands of one
region call, with its effective control context, MUST denote pairwise
disjoint wires (§3.3 disjointness); aliases violate it. **Static and
run-time verdicts.** Suite F rejects exactly: two `qreg` operands of one
region call or of one `qreg.concat` that are the same name; and, on some path from a
`qreg.slice`, a use of the view's name other than as the operand of one
region call (a `br`, `call` or `ret` transfer included), its reaching a
`ret` or the end of the function unconsumed, or a use of the slice's
parent operand name before that call. Every other overlap, and every
use of a viewed register through another name, is a CC trap / R fault
with code `FAULT_UB_TRAP` before any effect.

### 3.4 Execution in CC and R
### 5.6 Borrowed systems and borrow frames

**Requirement.** A region's completed operation equals its declared
operation ⊗ identity on every borrowed bundle and on any external system
entangled with it.

**Rule (suite F).** Borrowed wires (set B) are touched (as control or
target) only inside **borrow frames**: maximal contiguous permutation-
only subcircuits from the first to the last instruction touching B. The
frame's domain D_F contains every value of every borrowed and interface
wire, independently, and the values of its other wires admitted by the
entry-state analysis below, starting from the region's D (§5.2). The
frame's realised permutation P_F MUST satisfy
`P_F|span(D_F) = (P' ⊗ I_B)|span(D_F)` for some P' independent of B,
established by a T3 claim over all of D_F (exhaustive, or a library
certificate, §12 `borrow_frames`) or
**structurally by the translation lemma**: the frame is `V; W; V⁻¹; W`
where

- (i) every gate of V is an X-family flip (`x cx ccx mcx`) whose **target
  is in B** and whose **controls are all in a set X with X ∩ B = ∅**; V
  touches no other wire; hence V maps b ↦ b ⊕ v(X);
- (ii) every gate of W is an X-family flip whose **target is outside X,
  outside B, and outside the set of all controls of W** (as sets, over
  the whole of W), and W writes no wire of X;
- (iii) every gate of W has **at most one control in B**;
- (iv) V⁻¹ is the exact reverse sequence of V; no `swap` appears in the
  frame.

Then W's effect is affine in b, the net map is t ⊕= Σ_g c_g(Y)·v_{j(g)}(X),
independent of b, and b is restored (the dirty-ancilla MCX pattern).
Suite F contains the negative tests `cx b y; cx y z; cx b y` (V;W;V⁻¹),
`V = cx b2 b1, W = cx b1 t` (b2 ∈ B, violates X ∩ B = ∅), `V = cx b x`
(target outside B), and `W = swap y z; cx b y` (swap excluded). Oracles
are one frame verified by their own T3 claim over D.

For (i)–(iii), every gate's controls include its effective control
context, and V⁻¹ runs under the same effective control set and wire

### 14.2 Quantum state semantics

The state of a Q region is a vector in ⊗ over live wires in allocation
order (bundles little-endian). `qalloc` tensors |0⟩; `qfree` removes
clean factors; `qborrow` introduces a factor in unknown state possibly
entangled with a reference system R, and the region's operation must be
U ⊗ I on it (§5.6); gates act by §4.4 under the active predicate;
`measure` (CC) is the projective computational-basis measurement with
Born probabilities, consuming the wires.

### 14.3 Theorems

- **T1 (totality).** For every well-formed oracle, budget and x ∈ D the
  reference interpreter halts within B + 1 rows and F_B(x) is unique.
- **T2 (prefix reversal).** For every executor whose realisation is a
  composition of ε = 0 permutation gates implementing §5.2 steps 1–4
  with the inverse step of §3.6, on every x ∈ D every non-interface wire
  is clean after step 4 and borrowed bundles hold their initial values.
- **T3 (permutation theorem).** If the realised unitary V of a circuit on
  span(D) is a permutation matrix and the final basis tuple equals P(x)
  for every x ∈ D, then V|span(D) = P.
- **T4 (round trip).** For every finite CC/R trace with retained run
  history and tapes that is eligible for reversal under §10 — a
  `COMPLETED` or `FAILED` run containing no `measure` frame and no
  `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified — the history
  traversal of §7.4 restores the active environment, memory, allocation
  table, device-model state, the ledger entries of every register the
  run created or touched, token validity and active history (§7.4) to
  their entry values; audit records may remain; observed external
  events are not retracted. If, in addition, every region call is undone
  by the exact inverse of its realised forward operation (ε = 0
  realisations, or a realisation that is applied as its own adjoint),
  the quantum state of every register the run touched is restored
  exactly; otherwise to within the sum of the realisations' declared
  bounds (§12.3).
- **T5.** As §6.3.
- **T6 (executor agreement).** Two executors conforming to §3–§7 on one
  target with the same schedule produce on every x ∈ D the same final
  tuple for every oracle (exact) and, for every action with `operator_
  norm` inclusive bounds e₁, e₂, unitaries within e₁ + e₂ in operator
  norm; for channels, diamond bounds d₁ + d₂.

### 14.4 Mechanisation *(deferred)*

## 15. Conformance suites

The K82 named-interface vector is published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k82-09/`: the catalog's interfaces and invoke
edges, expanded leaf digests for those edges and accept/reject binding cases.

The K81 joint-operation / first-release vector is published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k81-09/`: both release orders, indivisible
inverse ownership and a duplicate-maximal cleanup selector tie. Its source
coordinates and ownership are explicitly finite-source-adapter data, not
a production 0.9 partition-builder result.

Portable K78–K80 vectors and reproduction instructions are published in
`conformance/fixtures/accepted-k78-k80-09/`, beside the controlled-action fixture.
The vectors record source admission, complete output tuples and expanded source coordinates; adapter-assigned coordinates are identified separately from production-builder output.

| Suite | Class | Tests |
|---|---|---|

**Suite H / §11.1 K77 adapter vector.** Captured `oracle_invocation`
follows the source call's arguments and body/template capture, irrespective
of unrelated apply-site context; nested calls restore the enclosing context
on exit. K77 conformance includes the same §16.3 `inc8` closure and
positional vector `[x,t,f]` captured with B=2 and B=3, forwarded through
`invoke`/`call` and captured body/template adapters under a conflicting
apply-site context. Nested calls use their own explicit tuples. Assert
frame counts 2 and 3, final `(t,f)=(x+1 mod 256,1)` from zero seeds,
exact inverse restoration, and retained forward `call_index` values during
inverse traversal (0/1 for the two calls within one source-row expansion;
separate source points each start at 0). Reject absent, malformed or
source-inconsistent records before concrete expansion. This vector tests
the existing K77 binding rule; it adds no catalog argument or binding rule.

126:        effect tape, branch-enumeration simulation,         proof-carrying output, exporters
148:| Quantum operations: allocate (clean / dirty / class), release, gate, rotate(θ), measure, quantum-addressed load and store | **Declared external marker functions** `__bennett_*` with fixed signatures, exactly as Enzyme's `__enzyme_autodiff`. Not `llvm.*` intrinsics (a plugin cannot register them). The plugin recognises them by name and signature at link time. **Positive legality requirement:** a marker is declared as an external function that may read and write memory and may not return (no `readnone`, `readonly`, `speculatable`, `willreturn`, `nounwind`-only combinations that would license elimination, commoning or speculation). Stock passes then preserve the *dynamic sequence* of marker calls on every executed path. | Containing functions may be inlined, cloned, unrolled or versioned, so the *static* count of marker call sites is not preserved; the external declaration itself is never inlined. *Explicit quantum registers* are accessed only through markers, never through LLVM `load`/`store`, so no alias-analysis or forwarding pass can touch them. The ordinary loads and stores of lifted classical code (all of SQLite's) are not quantum-register accesses: they are lifted coherently according to the incoming memory model, as classical memory whose contents happen to be superposed. This legality is a property of ordinary effectful external calls, not of any attribute we invent; the legality corpus (§3.2) tests it. |
153:| Control as structure: adjoint, controlled-by-qubit, compute-with-uncompute, reversible-tier entry | **Function granularity**: a region is outlined to a function (LLVM's `CodeExtractor`) and entered through a marker call `__bennett_ctrl(q, @f, budget, args…)`, `__bennett_adj(@f, …)`, `__bennett_compute(@f, …)`, or `__bennett_reversible(@f, budget, …)` for the reversible tier. The plugin generates controlled and adjoint bodies on demand from one source body. **Well-formedness:** a `ctrl`/`adj`/`compute` region is unitary: user measurement inside it is refused (or, when the front end asks for it, deferred by the deferred-measurement principle, which the certificate states); allocations inside are released clean or borrowed and restored; the compiler's own measurement-based uncomputation is the sole exception, and until its controlled form is fixed in the language's certificate kinds (§9.6) **unitary uncomputation is the fallback under control**. Exceptions and unwinding (`invoke`/`landingpad`, Rust panics, C++ `throw`) inside a region are step-machine control flow where the handler is in the region, and a fault code where it is not. | Front ends that want `when`-style syntax do the outlining themselves (Julia macro, clang attribute, Rust proc-macro) and emit the marker call; the dialect does not require it. Generating a body needs the callee's IR, which is why the plugin runs at link time (§3.3). |

     6	## Defect classification and North Star scope
     7	
     8	ISA 0.9 §5.2 includes every interface-region cell value in D. §12.2
     9	K69–K74 gives `proof` only for componentwise full D; successful exhaustive
    10	strict slices receive `evidence`. Dialect 0.3 draft §§19.9/25.2 correctly
    11	requests that existing evidence label. Assigning it is not a checker bug.
    12	
    13	North Star §5.1 calls exhaustive verification on a declared finite domain
    14	a proof, while §8 rung 4(a) fixes the SQLite database classically and
    15	varies all rows and all target values. This is a certificate terminology
    16	and scope mismatch, not missing SQLite execution semantics. The proposal
    17	adds a conditional proof without asserting correctness on other images.
    18	It retains all varying input, target, fault, dirty-system and inherited
    19	control components; neither sampling nor source edits replace the rung.
    20	
    21	## Alternatives and complete tiny witness
    22	
    23	| Choice | Consequence |
    24	|---|---|
    25	| Keep K74 | Current conformance; document the terminology mismatch. |
    26	| Call an image slice unqualified proof | Unsoundly promotes correctness to every database image. |
    27	| Checked image-relative proof | Recommended; the image is an explicit precondition. |
    28	| Put the database in a local image | Existing full-external-D proof for a different specialized closure and preparation route; does not relabel the original interface-region claim. |
    29	
    30	Let an exact, fault-free oracle at a sufficient bound have one input bit
    31	x, one target bit t, four fault bits f and one `in` region[1,8] db, with
    32	payload `db[x] & 1`. Its complete tuple relation is
    33	`(x,t,f,db) -> (x,t XOR (db[x]&1),f,db)`, workspace clean.
    34	
    35	For image `0102`, x=0 flips t and x=1 preserves it: payload `1 XOR x`.
    36	For `0201`, payload is x. Each image slice has 64 tuples; unrestricted D
    37	has 2^22 tuples, so coverage is exactly 1/65536. An implementation
    38	hardcoding `0102` passes the first 64 and fails all 64 for the swapped
    39	image. Fresh bindings and replay can prove the second image separately;
    40	its mere difference is not malformed input.
    41	
    42	An ordinary one-bit X action with no regions has domain t=0,1 and table
    43	0→1, 1→0. Its full-D exhaustive check must receive only `proof`, whether
    44	fixed_regions is absent or empty. This witness catches the first note's
    45	vacuously true image-relative condition.
    46	
    47	At A=16,W=8, with x width 10, t width 1 and f width 4, there are 15
    48	varying bits and 524288 fixed image bits. Coverage against unrestricted D
    49	is exactly 1/2^524288, never floating-point zero. Prepared cells remain
    50	interface components even though the experiment fixes them.
    51	
    52	## Recommended exact spec changes
    53	
    54	Extend the §12.2 domain schema and the certificate label-request/checker
    55	outcome enumerations; add a §0.2 K entry. Replace BOTH the introductory
    56	"otherwise evidence" rule in §12.2 and K74's blanket strict-slice
    57	classification with the exclusive classifier below. This is a proposed
    58	in-version certificate extension; no instruction or domain D changes.
    59	
    60	```text
    61	A domain MAY contain fixed_regions:[{name,A,W,image,sha256}]. An absent
    62	field is an empty list. name resolves by the existing component rules to
    63	an in-role interface region, with precisely the subject's A and W.
    64	Each resolved fixed region occurs exactly once and MUST NOT also occur
    65	in domain.regions. A mut-role region cannot be fixed by this mechanism.
    66	
    67	image is exactly ceil(2^A*W/8) raw bytes encoded as lowercase hexadecimal;
    68	unused high bits in the final byte MUST be zero. Cell i consists of bits
    69	[iW,(i+1)W) in the little-endian byte image. sha256 is SHA-256 of the raw
    70	bytes, not the hexadecimal text. The checker hashes independently and
    71	uses those cells on every replay. Fixed cells participate in domain
    72	validity and tuple/output checks, but each contributes one choice to n.
    73	The full unrestricted |D| remains unchanged. This metadata introduces no
    74	operation for inspecting unknown coherent state.
    75	
    76	After every §12.1 replay obligation succeeds, an exhaustive
    77	table/program/oracle claim receives exactly one outcome, in this order:
    78	(1) proof iff its domain equals unrestricted D component by component;
    79	(2) otherwise proof_relative_to_image iff fixed_regions is nonempty,
    80	    every image binding is valid, every fixed region is accounted for,
    81	    and every remaining component occurs exactly once with its full
    82	    range (all interface bundles/cells, borrowed positions and prepended
    83	    inherited controls included);
    84	(3) otherwise evidence.
    85	Successful sampled claims always receive evidence, including when their
    86	distinct draws cover the whole domain. Malformed domains, stale hashes
    87	and mismatches reject, rather than receiving any successful verdict.
    88	Omission-at-zero remains a strict slice unless the original component
    89	is thereby genuinely at its full range under the existing rules.
    90	
    91	Coverage remains exact n/|D| against unrestricted D, with distinct-draw
    92	coverage for sampling. The checker report identifies closure_hash,
    93	claims-array index, invocation/context key, and ordered resolved
    94	(region identity,A,W,image hash) bindings. A supplied label is a request,
    95	not a producer assertion. Requested proof rejects unless the computed
    96	outcome is proof; requested proof_relative_to_image rejects unless the
    97	computed outcome is proof_relative_to_image. An evidence request may
    98	accompany a stronger independently computed outcome, as before.
    99	
   100	K75's host-limit handling remains: unchecked (host limit) discharges no
   101	proof obligation and does not invalidate a legal domain or shrink it.
   102	```
   103	
   104	This deliberately preserves full-D K74 proofs and evidence for ordinary
   105	strict slices. The sole new successful classification is a nonempty,
   106	valid image binding with all other components fully quantified.
   107	
   108	```text
   109	A proof_relative_to_image theorem is conditional on the stated image.
   110	Using it to discharge an executable invocation additionally requires
   111	checked preparation/binding establishing that this in-region contains
   112	exactly those classical bytes and is factorised from other systems at
   113	entry, and that its exit image is restored. Establish preparation from
   114	declared bytes and logical preparation operations, never inspection of
   115	unknown coherent data. Without that preparation evidence the theorem
   116	remains conditional and does not discharge the invocation.
   117	
   118	This verdict cannot discharge an unrestricted borrow-frame, inverse-pair
   119	or other full-D obligation, and cannot be reused for another image,
   120	invocation, target realisation or schedule by discarding its bindings.
   121	All §12.1 exact +1 basis-phase, full-tuple, clean-workspace and
   122	external-reference obligations remain in force. A basis table cannot
   123	prove a non-permutation realisation; its numerical/channel obligations
   124	are unchanged. Target matching, resource accounting and error
   125	composition are not relaxed by conditional correctness.
   126	```
   127	
   128	For fixed image I, write J_I for the image-preparation isometry including
   129	prescribed clean workspace. The permutation assertion is `V J_I = P J_I`,
   130	also after tensoring with identity on an arbitrary external reference.
   131	When a process claim is needed, compare
   132	`E_real ∘ Ad_J_I` and `E_ref ∘ Ad_J_I` with the complete output interface.
   133	Controlled-unitary comparison retains a phase-sensitive bound on
   134	`||U J_I − V J_I||`; equal uncontrolled channels alone cannot discharge it.
   135	Numerical/channel acceptance procedures remain those of §12.1.
   136	
   137	## Mixed fat object (bennett-4ht)
   138	
   139	Add to §§9/10/12: "A native binary may carry VM objects only for marked
   140	modules. The host launch manifest selects a VM entry and records the
   141	classical image preparation/copy into its in-region, with resolved shape
   142	and raw-byte hash. The certificate inventory is that entry's selected VM
   143	closure and reached contexts, not the whole native executable. Image
   144	preparation may come from host database construction or a declared
   145	snapshot. Host bytes are bound to device cells using the declared packing,
   146	not host pointer identity. Unmarked main, argv/envp, libc and unrelated
   147	code remain native. The native comparison invokes the corresponding
   148	materialized marked entry over the same image and varying domain; it
   149	need not treat the entire binary as the oracle. Runtime shims and build
   150	configuration are recorded as required by the North Star."
   151	
   152	A preparation witness for this note is a host buffer `01 02` copied to
   153	the selected two-cell VM in-region before entry and retained unchanged
   154	through the oracle's return. Preparing an entangled region does not meet
   155	that classical-image precondition. This says how a mixed object uses the
   156	contract; a physical launch shim need not be implemented by this note.
   157	
   158	## Round-1 blocking items and resolution
   159	
   160	| Item | Change and concrete witness |
   161	|---|---|
   162	| sol XV4-B01: new rule overlaps full-D proof and K74 strict-slice evidence | Replace both blanket rules with one ordered, exclusive classifier; require nonempty fixed_regions. Full-D X receives proof, the 64-tuple 0102 slice receives proof_relative_to_image with 1/65536 coverage, sampling receives evidence. |
   163	
   164	The additional round-1 suggestions are retained: a complete oracle
   165	relation, stale-versus-fresh image mutations, explicit preparation-isometry
   166	comparison, and separation of requested labels from checker outcomes.
   167	
   168	## Hashes, publication and consumers
   169	
   170	Fixed images and requested labels change certificate bytes; assigned
   171	verdicts and resolved bindings are checker-report data. Under an
   172	authorized 0.9 K extension retaining the semantic envelope, §9.2 excludes
   173	certificate sections from object_hash, so source/interface/closure/object
   174	identities, targets, schedules and ref catalog remain unchanged. A full
   175	version bump instead requires regenerating version-envelope identities
   176	and dependent pins. This note authorizes neither publication choice.
   177	
   178	Producers/checkers must publish schema and verdict vectors together;
   179	legacy schemas must not silently treat the qualified spelling as proof.
   180	Until acceptance retain current evidence labels and exact fractions.
   181	The SQLite rung still requires unmodified SQLite, real SQL, quantum
   182	addressing, all rows/targets at N≤2^10, Grover at N≤2^8 and the named-target
   183	N=2^20 estimate. Conditional correctness does not replace those gates.
   184	xv4 remains open for acceptance and implementation.
   185	
   186	## Conformance vectors and verification
   187	
   188	In addition to the three exclusive-verdict cases: swapping 0102 to 0201
   189	while retaining the old digest, expected relation or preparation binding
   190	rejects reuse. Updating all bindings permits a fresh check, never reuse
   191	of the old theorem. Test hashing hex text instead of bytes, wrong A/W,
   192	nonzero padding, duplicate region identities, omitted target/fault/dirty
   193	ranges, partial controls, a requested unqualified proof and a mut-region
   194	binding. Identical image strings for two distinct resolved region slots
   195	remain separate bindings. Test outer controls 0/1, arbitrary dirty states
   196	and external references; sampled full coverage remains evidence.
   197	
   198	This exact finite witness checks hashes, the complete 64-tuples relations,
   199	exclusive classification premises and coverage. It is not execution of a
   200	new checker/schema, a SQLite run or council acceptance.
   201	
   202	```python
   203	from fractions import Fraction as F
   204	import hashlib
   205	import itertools
   206	
   207	images = (bytes([1, 2]), bytes([2, 1]))
   208	hashes = [hashlib.sha256(i).hexdigest() for i in images]
   209	assert hashes == [
   210	    'a12871fee210fb8619291eaea194581cbd2531e4b23759d225f6806923f63222',
   211	    '25dfd29c09617dcc9852281c030e5b3037a338a4712a42a21c907f259c6412a0']
   212	assert hashes[0] != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
   213	rows = list(itertools.product(range(2), range(2), range(16)))
   214	assert len(rows) == 64 and F(len(rows), 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
   215	for image in images:
   216	    for x, t, f in rows:
   217	        payload = image[x] & 1
   218	        after = (x, t ^ payload, f, image)
   219	        assert (after[0], after[1] ^ payload, after[2], after[3]) == (x, t, f, image)
   220	assert sum((images[0][x] & 1) != (images[1][x] & 1) for x, t, f in rows) == 64
   221	assert all((images[0][x] & 1) == (1 ^ x) for x in range(2))
   222	assert all((images[1][x] & 1) == x for x in range(2))
   223	# Full-D X: empty image set cannot satisfy the qualified predicate.
   224	full, fixed, remaining_full = True, [], True
   225	assert full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
   226	# Fixed-image slice: not full D, but every other component is full.
   227	full, fixed, remaining_full = False, [hashes[0]], True
   228	assert not full and bool(fixed) and remaining_full
   229	# Omit a target range: neither proof predicate holds.
   230	remaining_full = False
   231	assert not full and not (bool(fixed) and remaining_full)
   232	assert 2**16 * 8 == 524288
   233	assert F(2**15, 2**(15 + 524288)) == F(1, 2**524288)
   234	print('PASS xv4: two 64-tuple image relations; 64 swapped-image failures; exclusive proof predicates; exact coverage')
   235	```
