### 11.1 Reference-library contract (K11)

**ISA-REF-LIB** (K11; b-isa-spec-8r4, from bennett-vm-47t).
This is the decided 0.9 contract. The catalog bound as ref version 0.9
is the file `spec/ref-0.9.json` whose four-line envelope hashes to
`5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197`.
It publishes the complete 219-recipe catalog, byte-identical to the
accepted catalog snapshot. Consumers lacking the bound library report
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires. The witness below is not a
full-library hash.

**Serialization and identity.** The schema and family witnesses are
documented in `spec/ref-0.9-draft.README.md`. The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical
UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version`
and `recipes`. The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
`recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter
tuple order. Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or
other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable`
and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence). A parameterised recipe
uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the
instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation
operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`),
classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`,
`to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or
inverse (`inverse`, `body`). Constructor objects have exactly those
keys. Fully instantiated `operations` contain only emitted leaves, each
also carrying its computed `ordinal`; unexpanded `body` constructors
carry no ordinal. Scalars are integer literals, parameter names, or prefix arrays
# Bennett ISA reference interpreter

Rust 2021, standard library only. ISA 0.8 remains supported; ISA 0.9 migration
is in progress. Complete ISA 0.9 execution is not yet claimed. [PROGRESS.md](PROGRESS.md) records
what is verified, what is staged and in what order; [SPEC-QUESTIONS.md](SPEC-QUESTIONS.md)
records every reading the specification leaves open;
[council/response-round-1.md](council/response-round-1.md) answers the council's
round-1 review blocker by blocker. The crate is not yet a complete conformance
reference: the launch ABI (§10), target descriptions (§8), the schedule
consumer (§11) and the rigorous interval certificate checker (§12.1) are staged
as deliverables 8–11, and claims that need them are reported as unverified,
never as verified.

Build and test offline from the repository root (headless: `DISPLAY`,
`WAYLAND_DISPLAY`, `DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS`, `BROWSER` unset;
`TMPDIR=$PWD/reference-interpreter/.tmp`, created first):

```sh
cargo build --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline
cargo test --manifest-path reference-interpreter/Cargo.toml --offline -- --ignored
```

The executable is `reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref`. Data
commands write one JSON object to stdout and exit 0; `help`/`--help` prints
plain usage. Diagnostics go to stderr with exit status 1, and ISA diagnostics
cite their rule. Integers above 2^53 − 1 are decimal strings (§9.1).

> **Council status (2026-10-31):** accepted by the sol and codex seats in round 24 of run `isa-ref-2026-10-29` on snapshot ad84e75 (461 tests, five exhaustive sweeps; rounds 1–24 under `bennett/docs/council/isa-ref-2026-10-29/`). Staged deliverables 8–11 remain disclosed as staged. Muse seat held.

## Typed R faults and contract migration (ISA-7)

ISA 0.9 already resolves b-isa-spec-221: §0.2 K5, §4.1 `fault`,
§4.9 (implicit cause table, exact 64-bit terminal code, no successor or
later source effect, intact prefix history), §7.4, §10 (FAILED → REVERSED,
no result values, one fault record) and §14.3 T4. Zero maps to code 4;
nonzero codes, including program-defined codes, are preserved exactly.
This is a published 0.9 contract, not an amendment to accepted ISA 0.8.

The dialect 0.2.10 at `023f5a7` §11.5/§17.6 correctly reports
`blocked:BD-B-ISA7` for every R entry targeting ISA 0.8: the mandatory
pre-instruction source step check needs `fault 1` before the instruction,
with the same effect-tape prefix and reversible history. `--budget` here
is host execution fuel, not that source counter; generic `trap` cannot
implement arbitrary typed R faults. Closing the ISA wording issue does
not clear that consumer blocker or certify rung 3. Open follow-up
`b-isa-spec-fuk` retains that producer/executor migration prerequisite.

The reference migration has landed explicit 0.8/0.9 envelope, ABI, identity,
linking and device-version dispatch, plus K5 typed terminal faults and eligible
FAILED-run reversal on 0.9. `tests/typed_faults.rs` checks source-instrumented
pre-instruction boundaries (zero and returns included), exact uint64 codes,
implicit causes, intact prefixes and entry restoration. The source counter
remains producer instrumentation; host fuel is a separate diagnostic.

Bound `ref` expansion, end-to-end K79–K81 execution, the 0.9 saved-tape matrix
and the remaining authoritative delta (including dynamic qregs, minted tokens,
parametrised qinplace and nonempty lproc forwarding) remain open under
`b-isa-spec-fuk.3`–`.7`. The producer and sibling contract pins have not been
migrated here. The full musl hello-world, native equality, R console, reset
and saved-tape replay acceptance remains owed (North Star §8 rung 3;
dialect §19.2). The council status above applies only to its pinned snapshot;
a new milestone acceptance round remains the orchestrator's responsibility.

## Commands

`check <object>` parses and validates the object, computes identities, and
verifies every stored interface hash. Output: `object_hash`, `status`
(`"ok"`, or `"unlinked"` when imports are unresolved), `symbols` (each with
`interface_hash` and `closure_hash`, null when its closure has an unpinned
import) and `unresolved_imports`.

`hash <object>` produces the same identities without comparing stored hashes
(for building fixtures; it edits nothing).

`run-oracle <object> <oracle> --input <x[,y...]> [options]` executes the §14.1
interpreter and prints `{"code":0,"payload":9,"steps":3}` for inc8(8) at B=3.
Inputs of any width are decimal or `0x` hexadecimal; `--input ""` is the empty
list. The oracle name may start with `@`.

Options:

- `--budget B` or `--budget B,heap,stack,depth`. Default B is 1,000,000.
  Layout limits supply heap and stack defaults; depth defaults to 64 with a
  layout; memoryless limits are zero.
- `--mem name=hex` supplies an interface region's bytes in cell order;
  unspecified regions start at zero; the byte count must be exact.
- `--arg value` supplies the next classical argument: an integer,
  `f64:0.5`, or `token:B,heap,stack,depth` (after `--budget` the abbreviated
  token takes its limits as defaults).

`step-check <object> <oracle> MODE [options]` runs the independent §14.1
interpreter and the literal §3.6 machine on each domain element, compares their
F_B, then runs the B inverse steps and compares every location, cell, counter,
stack cell, allocator entry and frame with the entry tuple (T2, T6). The domain
is every input bundle and every byte of every interface region not fixed by
`--mem`; target and fault entry values do not affect the computation (XOR
delivery). MODE is one of:

- `--exhaustive [--max-bits N]`: the whole domain, refusing domains above the
  user's guard N (default 16; no fixed ceiling);
- `--suite`: the §17 item 13 default, exhaustive at ≤ 16 bits and otherwise
  2^16 xoshiro256** samples;
- `--samples N`: N samples, N ≥ 1 (`--seed a,b,c,d` sets the four state words).

Output: `cases`, `input_and_memory_bits`, `mode` (`exhaustive` or `sampled`),
`fixed_regions`, `status`. Default B is 256; `--budget`, `--mem` and `--arg`
apply.

The four commands below share these conventions. A classical value is
`{"type","value"}`: `iN` and `ptr` values are unsigned decimal strings of
their bit-vector values (a signed reading at width N is the caller's), `f64`
is a JSON number, `token` is an object with `steps`, `heap`, `stack` and
`depth` (§9.1 integers), and `qreg` is the register id. Fault records are
`faults`, a list of `{"code","name"}` with the §5.4 code and name (`name` is
null outside the §5.4 table), plus `fault`, that code or null. Tape entries
are `{"args","bytes","dev","op"}`: the §7.3 tape triple plus the bytes the
operation transferred, hex. Measurements are `{"bits","reg","value"}` with
`bits` in wire order (wire 0 first). Draws select the first outcome, in
lexicographic order of the bits string (wire 0 first), whose cumulative Born
weight exceeds draw × total weight; integer values remain little-endian
(SPEC-QUESTIONS 29). Console output is `console_hex` always
and `console` when it is valid UTF-8, else null. `memory_digest` is the
SHA-256 of the complete §7.1 memory (allocation table, heap bump, every
nonzero byte).

For CC/R entries, arguments bind positionally to the entry signature (§10, `E_ARG` on a type
or count mismatch): `--args a,b`, `--input a,b`, or repeated `--arg value`,
at most one form (omission means no arguments). An `iN`/`ptr` value is decimal (negatives accepted within
the signed range) or `0x` hex, range-checked at the width; `f64:` prefixes a
binary64; `token:B[,heap,stack,depth]` is a §5.4-validated budget token
(the four-component `--budget` supplies its defaults, as for `run-oracle`);
`qreg:<bits>` allocates a fresh register and prepares those basis bits
(first character is wire 0). `--entropy hex` is the entropy tape. `--draws
d,...` queues measurement draws in [0, 1). `--replies file` is a JSON array
of `{"service","payload","reply"}` objects (payload/reply hex) consumed in
HOST_REQUEST order: each request must match the next entry's service and
payload, every request must find a reply, and every reply must be consumed,
else the run is a §10 diagnostic and no JSON is printed. `--mem global=hex`
replaces a layout global's image in the entry state, at exactly its size.
For CC/R entries `--budget B[,heap,stack,depth]`'s first component is
execution fuel (CC/R has no ISA step budget; exhaustion is a §3.4
diagnostic, default 1,000,000) and the rest are token defaults.

`run-cc <object> <entry> [options]` runs a CC or R entry on the
`ClassicalMachine` and prints the complete run. Output: `entry`, `status`
(`COMPLETED` or `FAILED`), `steps`, `budgets` (one per `token:` argument, as
for `run-program`), `results`, `fault`, `faults`, `tape`,
`console`, `console_hex`, `clock`, `effect_tape_length`, `memory_digest`,
`host_requests` (`{"address","capacity","payload","reply","service"}`),
`measurements`, and `trace`. Each trace record is one executed instruction:
`step` (counting from 0), `function`, `block`, `index` within the block,
`line` (the object text line), `op`, `lhs`, `operands`, `registers` (each
`{"activation","name","pre","post"}`, the changed dynamic instances with
their previous and new current values, null when absent) and `memory` (each
`{"address","pre","post"}`, every changed byte). A hostcall record includes
its reply's deltas. A fault or trap is a `FAILED` result with no values,
not a diagnostic. This includes runtime region-interface and control/target
aliases created through dynamic SSA: the call records fault 4 before changing
quantum state or consuming a handle. Controls may share read-only inputs.

`reverse <object> <symbol> [options]` runs forward, then reverses, and
reports whether the complete entry state is restored byte for byte:
`equals_entry` and `first_difference` (null when equal, else a description of the first
differing component). `status` is `REVERSED` when equal and `FAILED`
otherwise; a reversal the ISA forbids is a diagnostic citing its rule. The
form depends on the symbol kind. For a CC/R entry, `reverse` takes the
`run-cc` options plus `--steps N` (reverse only after N forward
instructions; without it the whole run); it is §7.4 whole-run or prefix
reversal of the retained history. It also reports `memory_equals_entry`
(the §7.1 memory component). Output: `entry`, the forward `results`, `fault`,
`faults`, `budgets`, `steps`, `tape` and `host_requests`, then the post-reversal
`console`, `console_hex`, `clock`, `effect_tape_length`, `memory_digest`
and `entry_memory_digest`. A faulted prefix reverses like any prefix
(§7.4); a run containing `measure` is a §10 `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE` diagnostic.
For an oracle, `reverse` takes the `run-oracle` options (`--input`,
`--budget`, `--mem`, `--arg`) plus `--steps k` (default B, above B is a
§5.4 diagnostic): it runs k forward steps of the literal §3.6 machine, then
k inverse steps (§14.1 prefix reversal, T2). Output: `oracle`, `budget`,
`payload` and `code` of F_k as read off the machine, `steps` (its executed
rows), `forward_steps` and `reverse_steps` (both k), `frames_clean`, and
`state_after_reversal` with `pc`, `pc_prime`, `faulted`, `counters`
(heap_used, atop, sp, fp, depth) and `locations` (`function.name` to its
bit-vector value). For an action, `reverse` takes the `run-action` options,
executes the action and then its adjoint (§10 reversal of a top-level Q
call), and prints `forward_state` and the post-reversal `state`, `mut` and
`in`.

`run-action <object> <action> [options]` executes a Q action once (its
adjoint with `--adjoint`) on a basis state or a small state vector.
Exactly one input form: `--mut v,... [--in v,...] [--mem region=hex]` (one
integer per bundle, decimal or `0x`; `in` bundles and region cells default
to zero; region hex is one little-endian value per cell in cell order, of
exactly the region's byte count), `--basis <bits>`, or `--state
<bits>=<re>[,<im>][;...]`. Classical arguments take `--args` or `--arg` in
the `run-oracle` spellings. Bit strings and the output state use the
interface order: mut bundles, then in bundles, then mem regions with cells
in order, wire 0 of each bundle or cell first (bundles little-endian,
§14.2); `--basis`/`--state` bits cover that whole order. Output: `action`,
`adjoint`, `wires` (the interface wire count), `state`, `mut`, `in` and
`mem`. `state` maps each exactly nonzero component's bit string to
`[re, im]`, the binary64 values of the exact amplitudes (a component
appears iff its exact amplitude is nonzero; `--state` amplitudes are held
exactly as the dyadic values they denote). `mut`, `in` and each `mem` cell
give the exact integer when that bundle or cell is basis-definite on every
component, else null. Borrowed wires start in |0⟩ from the borrow pool and
must be returned to |0⟩ on every component, else the run is a §5.6
diagnostic.

`run-program <object> <cc-entry> [options]` is a one-shot §10 launch:
`bennett_load` (parse and stored-hash verification), positional
`bennett_run` of a device CC entry with the `run-cc` argument, entropy,
reply and draw options, result reporting, and `bennett_run_close`;
`--reverse` applies `bennett_reverse` before closing. The entry must have
kind `cc` (§10; other kinds are `E_ARG`). Output: `entry`, `status`
(`COMPLETED`, `FAILED`, or `REVERSED` with `--reverse`), `run_state`
(`CLOSED`), `reversed`, `steps`, `results` (a FAILED run exposes no values),
`fault`, `faults`, `measurements`, `tape`, `host_requests`, `budgets` (one
`{"steps","heap","stack","depth"}` per `token:` argument, §5.4-validated at
creation), `console`, `console_hex`, `clock`, `effect_tape_length`,
`memory_digest`, `entry_memory_digest`, and the final state's
`equals_entry`, `memory_equals_entry` and `first_difference` against the
entry state. On 0.9, `--reverse` also accepts eligible FAILED runs, removing
the terminal record and reversing the intact prefix. On 0.8, FAILED launch
reversal retains its `E_INVALID_STATE` diagnostic. A run that measured is
`E_NOT_REVERSIBLE` (§10). Two inputs are
explicit staged diagnostics, never silently ignored: `--target file`
(`bennett_load`'s target, staged deliverable 9: §8) and an object with a
`schedule` section (verbatim scheduled execution, staged deliverable 10:
§11). The rest of deliverable 8 (the full §10 C API as a library, estimates,
coverage) remains staged as PROGRESS records.

### Saved-tape profile v1

This section defines **saved-tape profile v1**, a versioned tool-level
contract, not an ISA interoperability MUST. Valid execution and reversal
for the same complete explicit inputs are determined by the ISA.
`bennett-vm` implements the same profile.

Record every successfully executed effect in execution order, including effects later cancelled by uncall; a faulting effect records nothing. Replay compares dev, op, integer args and transferred bytes with the deterministic candidate before committing. Mismatch or missing input leaves that step and cursor unchanged; unconsumed trailing entries reject. Reversal changes the active tape but does not erase the saved journal. The journal does not replace entropy, host replies, measurement inputs or entry state.

The saved file is a JSON array of objects with exactly the required keys
`args`, `bytes`, `dev`, `op` (no extra or duplicate keys). `dev` and `op`
are strings. `args` is an array of canonical unsigned integers fitting
u128: JSON numbers through 2^53 − 1, decimal strings above that boundary;
no leading zeros, signs, fractions or exponent forms. `bytes` is an
even-length hexadecimal string, including empty (readers accept either
hex case; writers emit lowercase). Writers
emit canonical JSON followed by LF; readers validate this schema strictly.

`run-cc`, `reverse` on a CC/R entry, and `run-program` accept:

- `--tape-out file`: after a successful command, write the forward effect
  journal as a canonical JSON `Tape[]` (schema below), followed by LF. Every
  successfully executed `effect` contributes its dev/op/args/transferred bytes
  in execution order. A faulting effect contributes no entry (§7.3 atomicity).
  For `reverse --steps N`, save only that forward prefix. Whole-run reversal
  does not erase the saved journal. A normal JSON `FAILED` run can be saved;
  replay-validation or reversal failure does not write a tape.
- `--replay-tape file`: read a saved `Tape[]` and consume its entries in order.
  The deterministic device model forms each candidate effect from the entry
  state and supplied entropy; its dev, op, integer args and transferred bytes
  must match the next saved entry **before the step commits**. A mismatch or
  missing entry rolls back that step (including memory, devices and history),
  and leaves the replay cursor unchanged. Extra entries at the end of the
  forward run/prefix also fail. Divergence exits 1 with a §7.3/§10
  `replay mismatch` diagnostic, no stdout JSON and no tape-output write.
  Malformed schema/hex/integer encodings and I/O errors are diagnostics too.
  Both options may be used together; each may occur only once.

Use the same object, entry, arguments, global images, entropy, measurement
inputs (if any), budgets/fuel, prefix length and checked `--replies` file.
Replay consumes the supplied tape during execution; it is not an unchecked
second run or a post-run comparison. Identical inputs and a valid saved tape
produce byte-identical stdout, including results, console bytes, trace, active
tape and reset report, with no added replay-only JSON fields. Entropy is still
supplied by `--entropy`; host request/reply payloads remain in `--replies`,
not in the device journal. This interface does not replace source R step
instrumentation. The complete 0.9 replay matrix remains migration slice 5.

The output JSON's `tape` remains the **active** §7.3 tape. Segment `uncall`
removes its cancelled entries from that active tape (§7.4); `--tape-out`
retains them in the execution journal so replay can validate them before their
cancellation. With no segment cancellation, the saved array equals the forward
output's `tape` and consumers may save that field directly. Otherwise use
`--tape-out`. A reversal command still reports the forward active `tape` and
post-reversal `effect_tape_length`, as before.

For the published five-effect device control (not the musl rung-3 corpus):

```sh
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-cc reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/classical.bisa effects --entropy 0b1621 --tape-out .tmp/effects.json
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-cc reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/classical.bisa effects --entropy 0b1621 --replay-tape .tmp/effects.json
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref reverse reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/classical.bisa effects --entropy 0b1621 --replay-tape .tmp/effects.json
```

Exact output schemas follow. This structural notation describes JSON:
`T[]` is an array, `Record<string,T>` an object with arbitrary string keys,
`&` combines object fields, and `|` is a union. Every listed field is
required; no other fields are emitted. `U` is a nonnegative integer,
a JSON number through 2^53 − 1 and a decimal string above it (§9.1);
`Decimal` is always an unsigned decimal string; `Hex` is an even-length
hex string; `Digest` is a 64-character lowercase SHA-256 hex string.
`number` denotes a finite binary64 JSON number.

```text
Budget = {steps: U, heap: U, stack: U, depth: U}
Value = {type: "iN" | "ptr", value: Decimal}
      | {type: "f64", value: number}
      | {type: "token", value: Budget}
      | {type: "qreg", value: U}
Fault = {code: U, name: string | null}
Tape = {args: U[], bytes: Hex, dev: string, op: string}
Measurement = {bits: string, reg: U, value: U}
HostRequest = {address: U, capacity: U, payload: Hex, reply: Hex, service: string}
Trace = {step: U, function: string, block: string, index: U, line: U,
         op: string, lhs: string[], operands: string[],
         registers: {activation: U, name: string,
                     pre: Value | null, post: Value | null}[],
         memory: {address: U, pre: U, post: U}[]}
State = Record<string, [number, number]>
Reported = {entry: string, steps: U, budgets: Budget[], results: Value[],
            fault: U | null, faults: Fault[], tape: Tape[],
            host_requests: HostRequest[]}
Effects = {console: string | null, console_hex: Hex, clock: U,
           effect_tape_length: U, memory_digest: Digest}
Equality = {equals_entry: boolean, first_difference: string | null}
Reversal = Equality & {status: "REVERSED" | "FAILED"}
MemoryEquality = {memory_equals_entry: boolean, entry_memory_digest: Digest}

run-cc = Reported & Effects & {status: "COMPLETED" | "FAILED",
                              measurements: Measurement[], trace: Trace[]}
reverse (CC/R) = Reported & Effects & MemoryEquality & Reversal
reverse (oracle) = Reversal & {
    oracle: string, budget: Budget, payload: U, code: U, steps: U,
    forward_steps: U, reverse_steps: U, frames_clean: boolean,
    state_after_reversal: {pc: U, pc_prime: U, faulted: boolean,
                          counters: [U, U, U, U, U],
                          locations: Record<string, U>}}
reverse (action) = Reversal & {
    action: string, adjoint: boolean, wires: U, forward_state: State,
    state: State, mut: (U | null)[], in: (U | null)[]}
run-action = {action: string, adjoint: boolean, wires: U, state: State,
              mut: (U | null)[], in: (U | null)[],
              mem: Record<string, (U | null)[]>}
run-program = Reported & Effects & Equality & MemoryEquality & {
    status: "COMPLETED" | "FAILED" | "REVERSED", run_state: "CLOSED",
    reversed: boolean, measurements: Measurement[]}
```

```sh
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref check reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/inc8.bisa
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-oracle reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/inc8.bisa inc8 --input 8 --budget 3
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref step-check reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/inc8.bisa inc8 --suite --budget 4
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-oracle reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/sum_db.bisa sum_db --input 3 --mem db=0102030405060708090a0b0c0d0e0f10 --budget 1000,4080,512,64
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-cc reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/classical.bisa sum --args 3
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref reverse reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/inc8.bisa inc8 --input 8 --budget 3
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-action reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/registers.bisa bell --mut 0
reference-interpreter/target/debug/bennett-isa-ref run-program reference-interpreter/tests/fixtures/registers.bisa measurement --args "" --draws 0.9
```

## Library

- `Object::parse` (grammar, schemas, §6 discipline, §5.6 borrow frames, §5.5
  matched cleanup, §5.1 call graph), `identity`, `check_hashes`, `Object::link`
  (resolution, pins, deduplication, certificate composition).
- `Object::run_oracle_vectors` is the §14.1 reference interpreter on
  `BitVector` values of any width; `run_oracle` is its checked u128 adapter.
- `OracleVectorMachine` is the literal §3.6/§3.7 machine with counters, cells
  and slots at the derived widths (`DerivedWidths`); `OracleMachine` is its
  checked u128 adapter; both expose `forward_step`, `inverse_step`, `commit`,
  `outcome` and complete snapshots.
- `ClassicalMachine` executes CC/R with dynamic SSA, retained history, the four
  §7.3 device models, tapes, hostcall request/reply, segment `uncall` and
  whole-run reversal; `QuantumRegisters` holds §3.3 qreg handles. It also
  exposes single stepping (`step_once`, `StepPoint`), the current `outcome`,
  the `effect_tape` with transferred bytes, `memory_digest`, caller-seeded
  globals (`with_quantum_registers_and_globals`), and snapshot comparison
  (`ClassicalSnapshot::delta`, `first_difference`, `memory_equals`); the
  `ClassicalSnapshot::effect_tape` exposes the checkpoint's active tape for
  transactional replay observers; the
  step machine snapshot likewise (`BasisVectorConfiguration::locations`,
  `counter_values`, `first_difference`).
- `QuantumState` is the §14.2 simulator: a basis fast path and a sparse
  superposition over any number of wires, each with an exact amplitude shadow
  in Q(√2, i)[z, 1/z] (`src/exact.rs`). Cleanliness, basis reads and state
  equality are exact. Born selection compares exact-shadow norm ratios using
  outward-rounded dyadic enclosures, refining without a precision cutoff.
  Supported outcomes survive underflow; conditional binary64 amplitudes are
  normalized with a shared extended exponent in both `amplitudes` and
  `exact_support`, including after further gates. Unprojected user vectors
  retain their supplied scale. Dyadic display conversion combines mantissa and exponent before rounding,
  including subnormals; algebraic cancellation refines exact enclosures before
  conversion when needed. Display probabilities remain
  approximations (a positive value can display as zero).
  `QuantumState::set_state_vector` installs a sparse superposition with each
  binary64 amplitude held exactly as its dyadic value.
  `Object::execute_action` runs structured Q bodies and their adjoints;
  `Object::borrow_capacity` sizes an action's §5.6 borrow pool.
- `Object::verify_certificate` and `verify_claim` re-execute exhaustive and
  sampled claims (`ClaimVerdict::Proof` only for exhaustive claims over D),
  check the complete named translation frame (canonical nested-line points,
  actual borrowed wires, exact reverse V, repeated W and side conditions) and
  library structural claims. Frame maximality inside qloop/qif is judged within
  the containing static body sequence (SPEC-QUESTIONS 30). Borrow-frame admission preserves every frame and
  completed return: definite violations cannot be waived by a region-final
  certificate. Both parsing and public certificate verification require
  permutation-only instructions touching borrowed wires, independently of tuple
  replay; unrelated quantum gates in an action remain legal. Expected programs bind bundles and region cells
  by ABI group and position, independently of local names; borrowed pools retain
  domain order and omitted partial-domain inputs start at zero. Interval
  claims and `expected: "oracle"` as `Unverified` with the reason.
- `Xoshiro256` is the §12.1 sampler.

Host limits are reported as such and are not ISA rules: a width or count above
2^64 − 1, a dense amplitude vector (`amplitudes`) or a region larger than host
memory, and B frames beyond the host address space fail with a "host limit"
diagnostic.

## Fixtures

Canonical `.bisa` fixtures in `tests/fixtures/` have adjacent `.comments` files
with hand-derived expected outcomes (comments are not a §16 extension). Every
fixture's stored hashes are audited (`tests/r1_fixtures.rs`), and the inc8 and
sum_db fixtures are compared byte for byte with §16.3.

### Round-2 static and display semantics

Function-local `qregion` declarations are established once at function entry,
including declarations inside zero-trip loops or after their first use. Each
lexical region name is unique. `qif` arms must restore their predicate stacks;
its adjoint reverses the complete expansion, including unconditional lifetimes
(SPEC-QUESTIONS 28). `qinplace` explicit adjoints exchange the oracle pair and
retain token and effective-control versions. Exchanges retain the dependency
union and other provenance on all outputs, and their inverse histories cancel
jointly. Borrow proofs distinguish the complete classical argument and token
bindings through inlined calls. Borrow/return lifetime boundaries survive loop
composition and adjoints: each return proves restoration and independence before
repetitions compose. A borrow spanning a loop may be restored by the whole loop;
a later borrowing cannot restore an earlier completed lifetime. Loop proofs
compose per-return obligations on reachable entries, retaining known-clean
workspace invariants.

**Design (round 10).** The §16.2 parser couples `fn` exclusively to CC/R
and uses the same header recognizer at function boundaries; Q kinds are bare.
A present bounds clause and an explicit LHS each require a nonempty list;
omitted bounds and empty operand lists remain legal. Exact source bytes are
hashed without normalization. Lexical predicate stacks retain their push/pop
order, while operation identities use a separate semantic effective-control
set: resolved scalar physical wires with polarity, sorted and deduplicated.
The §6 pass retains complete current version/dependency snapshots; borrow nodes
canonicalize after physical resolution and again after callee instantiation.
ABI input/output order, callee/token identity and all-operand LIFO matching
remain significant.

**Design (fact engine).** Per-wire ANF basis facts follow every gate: diagonal
operands and read-only controls retain their values, permutations transform
their ANFs, and non-diagonal targets become independent unknowns. Per-wire
operation stacks preserve nested compute/adjoint history: an exact adjoint
restores saved expressions only when the same event tops every operand stack,
so outstanding entangling readers block restoration. Basis-only permutations
need no history unless touching an existing stack. Opaque oracle calls and
qinplace join those same causal stacks: oracle_call is self-adjoint; qinplace
swaps g/ginv. Keys retain callee(s), token/classical specialisation, actual
input/memory/output wires and effective controls. Matched pairs restore saved
facts only at the top of every operand history; unmatched opaque readers block
outer restoration, and lifetime/import barriers invalidate connected history.
Assigning qinplace starts a history even on basis entries; loops containing it
use causal transfer rather than uninterpreted ANF assignment composition.
Source lowering publishes
physical bindings, live borrows and fact nodes through one executed structured
traversal: zero-trip bodies publish none of them; static validation and lexical
qregion hoisting still apply. Each loop visit resolves against its live entry
binding map and publishes its successor map. Equal-width, equal-role successor
lifetimes reuse entry slots only after the old lifetimes end (alpha-renaming).
Distinct environments get distinct transfers; recurring maps share an exact
structured cycle, without expansion proportional to the trip count.
Local action calls reuse a memoised per-wire
structured transfer per callee/classical specialization, instantiated on actual
wires and effective controls. Known-false controls disable fact transfer;
imports leave modified outputs unknown and preserve read-only facts, while
invalidating inverse history across all opaque readers. Existing
SCC validation excludes action recursion. Every quantum gate and non-permutation
call retains its physical frame boundary and full borrowed-touch guard, even
when disabled. Mixed loops visit every actual fact entry, skip repeated full
analysis states, or prove an inductive unknown-top invariant; pure permutation
loops compose exact maps and return obligations by squaring. Every return still
proves restoration and output independence. Certificates cannot override
definite lifetime or non-permutation failures. §6 cleanup readers use complete captured
version-stack prefixes, so older readers do not block a nested exact adjoint.
Round-9 literal pins and deterministic families retain all round 4–8 negatives
and add opaque pairs, unmatched readers and carried/reallocated loop bindings,
with independent identity maps including Bell-paired borrowed references.

 Workspace widths follow executed
producing/consuming lifetimes: zero-trip bodies have no lifetime effects (lexical
qregions are still hoisted), and every inverse loop visit recreates its own
matched producer's width. Structured width plans share repeated entry maps as
cycles without a trip-count limit. Round-6 pins include nested dead bodies,
qcopy/qload/qborrow variants, changing-width qalloc/qborrow loops and
alpha-renamed unrollings. Round-5 width evidence covers its tested straight-line
and self-contained loop lifetimes; the round-6 pins extend that evidence to
loop-carried width changes.
Matched lifted oracle calls preserve all tuple
boundaries and account for every possible numeric `qjumpr` destination.

Integer comparisons require integer or pointer operands; lifted memory width
suffixes use canonical INT spelling at load. State JSON selects support exactly
and approximates rotation phases using bounded finite factors, even when their
combined symbolic angle exceeds binary64. Numerical/channel certificate claims
still report the staged rigorous interval checker as deliverable 11.


## Design: kind, ownership and fixture evidence

Register files are kind-specific at load: oracle/lproc locations are reg[N],
CC/R use dynamic SSA, and action/perm_action workspace comes from Q producers.
An empty regs line remains legal and carries loop bounds. A named rotation
angle must be an f64 classical argument fixed at region entry (§3.1).

Oracle identity selects interface_regions by exact structured JSON owner key
in a lossless collection of original components, rendered path and value (§9.2).
Every selected structural leaf is emitted once, even when dotted renderings
coincide. Sort by rendered path bytewise, then by original components bytewise
(SPEC-QUESTIONS 31); dots remain legal. Every non-interface leaf remains in
the oracle envelope. Lprocs select exact global keys and their images; actions
and data retain their existing closure exclusions.

Identity classifies references by instruction and operand context in both the
dependency graph and envelope replacement. A declaring oracle's lift.addr
interface operand stays literal even if its spelling names a function, data
symbol or import. Calls, qregion images and actual globals remain symbol
references. Only the separate global/interface address ambiguity retains its
existing rejection (SPEC-QUESTIONS 32).

Fixture hand derivations must distinguish exact semantic support, rounded
diagnostic amplitudes and token preconditions. A finite binary64 angle is
never symbolic pi. Probability-one interference uses an exact discrete
minus-I action; the rounded-angle probe must retain both support keys.
qinplace identity requires a fault-free token; B=0 is a §4.5 rejection.
Literal envelopes and independent hashlib pins precede interpreter checks.
The complete round-11 sidecar audit is in tests/support/r11-comment-audit.md.

**Design (metadata, round 17).** `src/metadata_schema.rs::TABLE` is the
single declarative recursive grammar for all seven JSON metadata sections,
signatures and the textual data NAME/HEX projection. Every production cites
its clause (§9.1, §9.2, §9.4, §8.1, §11, §12.1–12.3, §13 or §17 items 7, 9 and 13). Both the
pre-identity admission pass and certificate/claim admission use this table;
there are no parallel hand-written field/type schemas. Y endpoints are
signed finite decimal strings; exhaustive tables contain arrays of canonical
§9.1 integers of arbitrary size. The translation frame is a two-point tuple
in the grammar; V and W remain point arrays. Schedule `bindings.expansion`
is optional under §17 item 7: omission binds `ref` at the object's ISA version
(0.8 or 0.9). A present descriptor retains the closed name/version/hash
grammar. `Object::schedule_expansion` resolves that binding for consumers,
returning the published full-library hash for the 0.9 default, retaining the
legacy absent hash for 0.8, and preserving an explicit binding.
It never inserts a descriptor into the canonical schedule or changes its §9.2
hash. `Object::bound_ref_library` resolves the available 0.9 library with the
conformance crate's Rust envelope check; `RefLibrary::expand` reuses its typed
K19–K28 constructor engine with a fresh source-bound registry. `OracleInvocation`
captures and validates a K77 record against a resolved source budget, retains
its positional actuals, and calls a supplied body builder per invocation.
These APIs do not implement general linked-source lowering or automatic
capture through source arguments/body/template adapters. Full .3 acceptance
also awaits K13 invocation/K16 frame admission for the unmodified published
controlled-action certificate; removing that certificate is not acceptance.

`gate.epsilon_share` is optional under §§8.1/17 item 9, including
realisation-only entries. `Object::schedule_epsilon_share` preserves explicit
shares or returns the exact region-epsilon / approximate-operation-count
recipe after expansion supplies that count; exact operations stay exact.
Admission never inserts a numeric share or rounds a repeating fraction.
`sampler.count` is optional under §17 item 13; claim replay supplies 65,536
when absent, and validates every present count through the same grammar.
Target/schedule execution remains staged. Semantic checks remain in
`object.rs` (layout ranges, image resolution), `metadata.rs` (schedule paths,
payload agreement and maximal-match ambiguity, exact decimal-value zero for oracle epsilon;
coverage points), `certificate.rs` (domain bindings,
indices, expression environments/arity, live-range overlap and certificate
bindings), and `validation.rs` (device model compatibility). These checks
cite their determining clauses. Target/launch/schedule execution and interval
verification remain staged. Arbitrary string service versions and complete
sector block images retain their forms. The grammar-derived family in
`src/metadata_schema/tests.rs` replaces the R13 path/type union: it populates
nested arrays/maps, tests every production and alternative, walks all supplied
paths, and rejects malformed types before hashing or claim execution.
Round 17 observes 163 productions, 9,897 generated positives, 1,653 unique
typed paths and 26,746 grammar negatives (1,992 also through both admission
validators); 26 pinned objects add 2,393 parse visits and 17,173 parse negatives.
This tests enforcement of the table; independent clause-derived controls
check frame cardinality, numeric zero and omitted defaults through full
admission. `r17_section17_defaults_are_optional_and_applied` independently
enumerates all 14 §17 items, distinguishing metadata defaults from decisions
without a metadata field and staged target/interval consumers.

**Design (schedule matching, round 18).** Specificity is the §11 lexicographic
tuple; only ties at the actual maximum invalidate a schedule. Admission
intersects equal-specificity selectors with known invocation/loop domains,
then subtracts the union of all strictly higher matching domains of that kind.
A nonempty remainder is a concrete maximal-tie witness. Step bounds follow
the owning invocation: the root oracle uses bindings.budget[0]; lift.call and
lifted action gates stay in that machine. lift.oracle_call/uncall and normalized
oracle_call enter a new token-governed invocation, never bounded by root B.
ISA 0.8 has no object-level token constant: every nested budget is a classical
parameter, unavailable from params_hash. The derivable-nested-budget branch
therefore cannot fire at admission in 0.8; whether 0.9 adds one is a spec
decision (b-isa-spec-6hs). Admission checks root-owned domains exactly and
**defers** unknown nested domains. Neither root clipping nor a symbolic
"any budget" nested rejection is sound (INV-5 1.4.3; SPEC-QUESTIONS 33).
`Object::check_schedule_matches_for_run` binds the actual tokens, including
callee parameter forwarding, and uses their own budgets in the same domain
algorithm. For each concrete ambiguity it calls `Object::resolve_schedule`,
which counts final maxima independently of document order and raises the §11
diagnostic before any realisation. This matching preflight runs before either
oracle engine or action execution mutates state; it is not full target/timing
execution. A valid token-B=2 run is never rejected for a tie only at step 3.
Domains split at selector boundaries; step/loop bounds are never expanded or
capped. Known finite step domains require checking call extensions that can
enter a larger invocation. Suffix states (including bound arguments and budget)
are memoized beyond the longest selector path, so recursive lprocs terminate
without a depth cap. Empty paths split over real root instructions; zero-trip
loops, root B=0 and zero-budget nested ancestors stay empty. Generated ordinal
ranges remain symbolic for the staged expansion library.
The retained R17 family checks 1,200 schedules and reversed orders against 54
concrete selector partitions each. The R18 family adds 7,500 schedules and
reversed orders over depth 1–3 oracle/lproc chains and action roots. It covers
1,532 distinct concrete budget assignments, enumerating 569,910 operation
partitions. Its oracle checks root-only admission separately from tie
reachability under **each actual run's budgets**, and compares bound matching
and concrete resolver results, including deferred errors: 100% agreement.
These finite selector families do not claim full schedule/target execution.
Union/partial coverage, parameter forwarding, and zero/large loop bounds have
separate controls.

**Design (round 20).** `const_literal` validates canonical decimal magnitude
using storage proportional to its spelling, then compares bit length with N;
negative literals at the signed minimum additionally require a single set bit.
It never constructs N+1 bits or adds to N. The same magnitude check serves
fault codes and certificate endpoints; admission of small constants/intervals
at huge widths needs no destination-sized storage. `Function::signature` emits
large dimensions as decimal strings under §9.1. Concrete wire names, region
images/cells, quantum identities, claim elements and selected CLI interface buffers
received fallible reservations, with checked products/cardinalities. Range errors
cite their ISA clause; representation/allocation failures additionally name the
host limit, without an ISA width cap.

`DerivedWidths::from_program` checks H+2, A_tab+1 and S_cells+1; a legal layout
whose counters exceed host representation returns a cited host-limit error.
Admission and sequential B=0 semantics remain independent of that host limit.
The depth addition and bit-vector limb/storage sizing also use checked
arithmetic. The R13 family retains its 507 inputs and adds the 2^63 boundaries
(591 cases), assigning expected layout/range, deterministic representation,
legal and allocation-dependent outcomes before execution. Its R20 siblings
cover text descriptors, casts, counts, bounds, region products, storage,
schedules and certificate **admission** domains, plus selected direct enumeration
consumers: 102 fixture-qualified paths / 1,113 cases
total, with catch_unwind and cited range/resource distinctions. This is a
finite extreme-value sweep. It missed executable all-cells domain expansion
and classical transfer lengths; it was not a complete storage-safety audit.

**Design (round 21).** `claims::Object::domain` checked-adds and fallibly reserves
the cumulative all-cells component count before expanding 2^A. The current
replay engine materializes every region cell, so this repair retains the eager
domain representation and reports a §12.2 host limit when it cannot fit. A
count=1 sample does not imply that its complete input state fits the host.
Admission and the reviewer hashes are unchanged, with no ISA A/W/count cap.
Both the all-cells and explicit-list branches reach checked replay storage.
Nested oracle region transfers and memory loads reserve before iteration.

Classical hostcall (§10), console.write (§7.3), entropy, reply and undo buffers
reserve before filling; cumulative console storage and the effect-tape entry
reserve before device mutation. A failed hostcall publishes no request; a
failed console transfer appends neither output nor a tape entry, and the
instruction prefix reverses exactly. Sparse allocation remains legal through
the pointer range. Invalid ranges and exhausted entropy retain their ISA fault
semantics. Qreg slicing/concatenation and CLI hex output check derived storage;
trace records reserve incrementally, and step-check retains its lazy odometer.

The R21 extension covers **24 tagged consumer paths / 186 generated cases**,
separately from the retained R20 family (the path sets overlap). It distinguishes
admission, exponent-derived cardinality, concrete materialization and sparse
or lazy consumers. Exponents include 62/63/64 and 127/128; lengths include
2^63−1, 2^63 and 2^64−1, with successful small controls. Resource regressions
use catch_unwind inside fresh Linux subprocesses with RLIMIT_AS=256 MiB and
RLIMIT_CORE=0; an allocator abort cannot be caught by catch_unwind alone.
Full reviewer objects, independent pins and public verify_claim /
verify_certificate / library / CLI consumers are retained in
`tests/fixtures/r21`, `tests/r21_resources.rs` and `tests/support/r21_pins.py`.
The huge CLI sample-count probes stop at a downstream frame-storage error;
they test lazy entry, not completion of huge replays. Resident collection sizes
and general allocator exhaustion are not a full-ISA resource-availability proof.

**Design (host-request retention, round 22).** Initial transfer storage and
history-entry capacity are reserved before publication. A `HostRequest` owns a
shared immutable descriptor; its payload, retained reply, undo bytes, finished
values, console and tape use `Retained<T>` (`Arc<Vec<T>>` plus a visible length).
The reserved vector moves into its owner: converting a `Vec` to `Arc<[T]>` would
copy the payload again. Pending/history/outcome/transcript and snapshot clones
share payloads. Sparse resident memory is a sorted, shared entry vector; a
snapshot never copies transferred resident bytes. Writes and copy-on-write
append reserve before mutation; truncation for reversal is a shared prefix.
A reply prepares its reply bytes, overwritten-byte history, sparse memory and
result retention before publishing any change. Frame capacity is reserved
before the instruction. No transfer-length or ISA limit is introduced.

Observer allocations are explicit Results: `host_transcript`, `memory_digest`
and snapshot `delta`; `host_exchanges` borrows entries. CLI hex/UTF-8 conversion,
trace growth/deltas and final `Json::try_canonical` serialization reserve
fallibly. The latter writes into one buffer instead of cloning, formatting and
joining payload strings. The legacy `Json::canonical` remains for metadata and
identity callers; executed CC/R payload output uses the fallible API. Structured
`ErrorKind::HostResource` preserves diagnostic Display text and context;
`Error::new` replaces the old one-field tuple constructor. ISA faults remain
outcome fault codes. `ClassicalOutcome::values` and request/reply/tape bytes are
immutable `Retained` slices; callers can borrow via Deref/AsRef. Snapshot payload
sharing does not claim allocation-free copies of unrelated control/quantum
metadata, nor recovery from every fixed-size allocator bookkeeping failure.

The retention/later-copy extension has **12 tagged stages / 53 stage cases**
(overlapping path observations, not 53 distinct programs), including a legal
64 MiB request under RLIMIT_AS=128 MiB, an 8 MiB reply whose subsequent undo
reservation is denied, denied console retention after its initial transfer,
and denied final JSON storage after its first 64 MiB buffer. Errors are typed,
clause-cited and leave the instruction/reply prefix unchanged; machine cases
reverse exactly. The 0/1/8-byte and moderate successful transfers and original
2^63 rejections remain. New Linux children disable core dumps and set
MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=1 to keep unrelated glibc arena reservations from hiding the
required allocation stage. Complete pinned witnesses are in `tests/fixtures/r22`.

**Design (claim coverage, round 22).** Exhaustive coverage is checked over
resolved components: a `Bundle(group, slot)` covers only that interface bundle,
a region is complete only when its own cells all have full ranges (either
`cells:"all"` or a complete explicit list), and a borrowed component has its
own ordinal. A borrowed name never supplies a missing interface component.
This follows [INV-6 §1.3.4](../../bennett-conformance/proofs/INV-6-certificate-soundness.md)
(and §1.3.5/§1.5.5): renaming borrowed entries cannot change coverage or the
verdict. Duplicate spellings are checked within roles, allowing names shared
across roles. The supplied CNOT and table spoofs now yield Evidence; the
complete CNOT domain still rejects at element 4. A 32-case public replay family
checks exact-label invariance under borrowed renaming; 256 direct coverage
cases also cover regions and complete/partial explicit cell lists. These fixes
do not claim to discharge INV-6's separately documented specification readings.

| Schema / rule | Load-time validator | Positive and negative controls |
|---|---|---|
| §9.1 optional `layout.block_image`, §7.3 sectors | `metadata_schema::TABLE` (`layout`), `Object::validate_layout`; checked lookup in `ClassicalMachine::construct` | `parser::r13_block_image_schema_is_checked_without_a_layout_consumer`, `r13_classical_missing_block_image_never_panics`; absent/disk action identities in the R13 pin corpus |
| §9.1 optional `imports.pinned_closure_hash`, §9.3 bindings | `metadata_schema::TABLE` (`import`), `Object::validate_symbols` | `linking::link_resolves_calls_and_pins_and_deduplicates`, `link_rejects_unresolved_signature_pin_definition_and_layout_conflicts`; R14 derived type family |
| §9.1 coverage classification payloads, §13 | `metadata_schema::TABLE` (`coverage_entry`), `Object::validate_coverage_details` | `metadata::coverage_points_and_classification_payloads_are_checked`; all three classification branches in the R14 derived type family |
| §9.1 devices, layout and every accepted schedule/certificate optional field | `Object::validate_metadata_types`, followed by the section's semantic validator | `metadata_schema::tests::r14_generated_metadata_schema_type_family`: literal pinned controls and one-field type substitutions |
| §§3.7, 7.2 derived counters and storage | `DerivedWidths::from_program`, `BitVector::zero` | `r1_widths::r13_generated_derived_width_extremes_family`; recursive S_cells=9 reversal in `step_machine` |

These admission checks do not claim the staged target, launch, schedule
execution or interval-verification deliverables. Borrow obligations remain
separate: `validation.rs::validate_action_sequence` checks static cleanup and
causal histories, `borrow.rs::decide_frames` checks full-frame factorisation,
and `quantum.rs::audit_borrow_boundary` checks runtime return obligations.


**Design (transactional steps, round 23).** `ClassicalMachine::step_once` and
`reply` publish their memory, control, devices, pending request and retained
history together. Execution prepares a private candidate while a checkpoint
retains the pre-write shared sparse/console/tape owners. On any returned error,
restoration moves those owners back and truncates candidate history; it never
replays byte undo or asks an allocator to recover from a denied allocation.
Nested `uncall` also checkpoints edited history flags and segments; measurement
checkpoints the draw queue and RNG. Committed frames still retain byte deltas,
not a complete memory image per frame. Observer payload clones remain shared;
no new payload copy or ISA size limit is introduced. Sparse COW prepares final
capacity once and reserves growth only for new resident addresses.

After `HostResource`, the full snapshot, history length, console/effect tape and
host transcript equal the pre-call prefix. Retrying executes the same opcode;
reply rejection leaves the same pending request. `step_once_observed` and
`reply_observed` prepare fallible trace data within this boundary; CLI trace
records and reply-queue consumption publish only on success. Observer callbacks
should return prepared values and publish external side effects after success.
See [`r23_transaction`](tests/r23_transaction.rs) for both pinned 128 MiB COW
witnesses, unchanged-prefix/repeated-denial checks, raised-limit retry and entry
reversal, and [`src/r23_transaction.rs`](src/r23_transaction.rs) for stage-indexed
failure injection across instruction, reply, trace and certificate preparation.
These are explicit Result-path guarantees, not recovery from Rust's infallible
metadata allocation aborts. All round 4–22 tests and 64 MiB controls remain.

The R23 transactionality family has **533 indexed stage cases / 70 operation
scenarios / 31 stage tags**: 340 instruction-stage cases, 27 reply/observer,
7 standalone trace-observer, 144 immutable certificate-replay and 15 concrete
CLI trace/reply cases. Each case rejects twice at the same stage, preserves the
prefix (and CLI trace/reply queue where applicable), then succeeds after the
injection is disabled. Counts refer to preparation boundaries, including
capacity checks that may reuse storage, rather than physical allocator calls.
Successful stores/loads additionally check literal byte/value semantics;
reversible controls restore entry. Measurement remains irreversible. These
counts are separate from the retained R22 12-stage/53-case transfer inventory.
The public coverage extension adds 56 oracle-role/explicit-region-cell cases.

### 19.9 Options, identity, outputs, module flag

| Option | Meaning | Default |

**Identity.** `llvmGetPassPluginInfo` returns plugin name `bennett` and version string
`dialect-0.3;isa-0.9`; the driver refuses a plugin whose string differs. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23] One plugin build per LLVM
major (NS §3.2).

**Outputs** (in `-bennett-out`, file names plugin-defined, §24 D22): the materialised device
variants as bitcode (§17.7); the host module with device entries replaced by launch stubs; the ISA
objects (ISA §9, §16); the certificate (ISA §12) with `producer.materialisation = {llvm_version,
pipeline, normaliser_version: "0.2.8"}` and `bindings.dialect_mapping_version = "0.3"`; the coverage
report (ISA §13), whose `source` field is `dialect-0.3:<rule id>` (e.g. `dialect-0.3:N33`); the
lowering reports (§25.1); and the **build record**, one JCS object with the build flags, the
budget table (resolved, including each shape's selection_identity, final semantic identity,
resolved components and frozen layout capacities under §9.2) and its SHA-256, the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes, the snapshot
hash, the layout options and the `cpuid` leaf table (§15.2). Its SHA-256 is recorded as the build metadata of the certificate's
`producer.version` (`<driver version>+build.<hex>`) because ISA 0.9 has no dedicated field for it
(§25.2). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2] The certificate MUST bind the full invocation and explicit expected relation under ISA 0.9, retain build-record and resolved-table identities and deferred-measurement information in the existing documented metadata channel where no dedicated field exists, and label a fixed-region subdomain as evidence with its covered fraction. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2, DLT-18.7, ISA-12.1-P7, ISA-12.2-P1, ISA-12.3-P2, ISA-12.3-P3] Labels are the checker's (ISA K74): a producer label is a checked request.

**A missing plugin fails loud.** No production artefact defines a marker: `bennett-runtimes`,
`bennett-arith` and the driver's link inputs define no `__bennett_*` marker symbol (test runtimes
such as the trace runtime do, and are never linked into production). A link in which the plugin
did not run therefore leaves undefined references and fails at link time; the driver MUST NOT
ignore unresolved symbols or supply weak marker definitions.
### 25.2 Wording items

Every 0.2.x wording item has its answer in ISA 0.9, which this draft adopts:

| 0.2.x item | ISA 0.9 answer |
|---|---|
| ISA-8 R fault status and reverse eligibility | K5, K6: R faults are terminal, no status reaches a CC caller, `FAILED` runs reverse |
| ISA-3 editorial, `args` JSON spelling | E16.8 |
| `qreg.slice` run-time `lo` | E12 |
| `qreg` across CC calls | E11 |
| code 10 in oracles | E13 |
| certificate fields for the build record, budget table and `deferred_measurement` | no dedicated field; the metadata channel below is the contract (K13 binds the invocation and expected relation) |
| fixed classical regions in §12.2 domains | K69–K74: a strict subdomain is evidence with its covered fraction; the checker owns the label |

Certificates MUST retain build-record and resolved-table identities and deferred-measurement information in the existing documented metadata channel where no dedicated field exists, and a fixed-region strict subdomain MUST be labelled evidence with its covered fraction by the checker, treating a producer label as a checked request. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2, DLT-18.7, ISA-12.1, ISA-12.2, ISA-12.3]

### 25.3 Efficiency items

accepted catalog snapshot. Consumers lacking the bound library report
`E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires. The witness below is not a
full-library hash.

**Serialization and identity.** The schema and family witnesses are
documented in `spec/ref-0.9-draft.README.md`. The JSON file is RFC 8785 canonical
UTF-8, with no trailing LF, containing exactly `isa`, `name`, `version`
and `recipes`. The first three are strings `0.9`, `ref`, `0.9`.
`recipes` is an array in bytewise `kind` order, then increasing parameter
tuple order. Each instantiated recipe has `kind`, its integer width or
other named integer parameters, `operations`, `scratch`, `removable`
and `source` (the motivating ISA sentence). A parameterised recipe
uses `parameters` (an ordered array of formals defined below) and `body` instead of the
instance's `operations`; its body uses the closed constructors below.
Recipes cover every §4 instruction/row, the §3.6 step, §3.7 activation
operations and §5.2 construction, with no implementation-private leaf.
Classical instructions/effects remain classical leaf events (§8.2),
not invented quantum gates. The README documents schema, parameters,
coverage and witnesses; it is not hashed and cannot alter operations.

The library hash is SHA-256 of this UTF-8 envelope (every line ends LF,
including the last); the fourth line is the **entire** canonical library
JSON, including all recipes, sources and versions, without an embedded
hash field:

```
isa=0.9
expansion=ref
version=0.9
<JCS library document>
```

`bindings.expansion` names `ref`, version `0.9`, and this 64-lowercase-hex
hash. This adopts §9.2's version-prefixed, LF-terminated **envelope
convention**, with explicit expansion-domain and library-version lines;
it is a new library rule, not §9.2's symbol signature/SCC envelope.
Motivation (0.8 §11): "a versioned document, identified in the schedule's
`bindings.expansion` as { name, version, hash }"; 0.8 §9.2: "Semantic
envelope ... every part ends with LF". A changed recipe, source, scratch
name or ordering changes the library hash; no schedule/certificate can
silently inherit that change (§12.1's realised-subject binding).

**Expansion and ordinals.** Recipe bodies are ordered arrays
of constructors: a gate (`gate`, ordered `wires`, optional `params`), an
allocation (`alloc`, `width`, `class`, `state`), release (`release`),
classical event (`classical`, `point`), counted loop (`for`, `from`,
`to`, `body`), named recipe expansion (`invoke`, ordered `args`), or
inverse (`inverse`, `body`). Constructor objects have exactly those
keys. Fully instantiated `operations` contain only emitted leaves, each

# ref 0.9 constructor catalog — complete under draft 0.9

Published byte-identically as [ref-0.9.json](ref-0.9.json) after council acceptance (2026-10-31); the status below records the draft completion checkpoint.

Status: **complete under draft 0.9 — 219 recipes; publication remains gated
on accepted 0.9**. Every row in the disposition table below is bound.
The complete-library hash/count is recorded once in draft §11.1. No ref/
publication or schedule execution is claimed. Historical checkpoints and
family counts remain in [the progress file](ref-0.9-draft.PROGRESS.md).
No interpreter source was read or copied into the checker.

The document is RFC 8785 canonical UTF-8 without a trailing LF; recipes are
ordered by bytewise kind. Every recipe has kind, ordered parameters, body,
scratch, removable:[], and its motivating ISA source. Typed formals,
constructors, lexical capture, call ordinal spaces and fresh allocation
identities are defined by §11.1 K19–K28. An image actual binds exact data
bytes; empty means absent/default-zero even for non-byte-aligned regions.
Template application binds explicit frame/wire actuals while preserving free
captures. Arguments vectors are tied to one closure hash and capture their
actuals' environment; value formals preserve exact §12.3 integer, ptr, f64
and token encodings. K25–K27 add ordered descriptors, source region roles/shapes/origins and shared allocation declarations.

The generators under conformance/tools/ contain no interpreter imports.
Run ref_catalog.py with python3 -I (the required headless project TMPDIR
environment applies). Python's independent integer-subset JCS canonicalizer
checks the printed qxor witness and hashes the four-line envelope. Rust's
independent canonicalizer and SHA-256 must agree with it. Source/scratch
names, bound images and constructor order are hash-significant.

//! §12 claim checking: re-execution of exhaustive and sampled claims on the
//! reference semantics, and the syntactic side conditions of structural claims.
//!
//! Encodings the specification leaves open are fixed in SPEC-QUESTIONS 21:
//! a domain element's output tuple is the region's final interface in
//! signature order (oracle: ins, tgt, fault, in-region cells; action: mut
//! bundles, in bundles, region cells in signature order), then the borrowed
//! components; `expected.table` lists one tuple per domain element in
//! enumeration order, each component a canonical decimal (JSON number when
//! at most 2^53 - 1, else string). Interval-arithmetic claims (`numerical`,
//! `channel`) and `expected: "oracle"` (which compares a realisation under a
//! schedule) need the target/schedule executor and are reported as
//! `Unverified` with that reason, never as verified.
use crate::{
    err, json::Json, BitVector, Budget, Error, Function, Object, QuantumBindings, QuantumState,
    Result, Type,
};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

/// §17 item 13: an explicitly sampled claim defaults to 2^16 samples.
/// This is a consumer default, never a mutation of certificate JCS.
pub(crate) fn sample_count(sampler: &Json) -> Result<u128> {
    crate::metadata_schema::validate("sampler", sampler)?;
    sampler
        .object()?
        .get("count")
        .map_or(Ok(1 << 16), Json::uint)
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum ClaimVerdict {
    /// Re-executed over D (or the structural rule holds with verified subclaims).
    Proof,
    /// Re-executed over a domain that is not all of D (or sampled).
    Evidence { elements: u128 },
    /// Not checkable by this crate yet; the reason names the missing machinery.
    Unverified(String),
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ClaimReport {
    pub region: String,
    pub index: usize,
    pub kind: String,
    pub verdict: ClaimVerdict,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
enum Component {
    Bundle(usize, usize),
    Borrowed(usize, String),
    Cell(usize, usize, usize),
}
struct Domain {
    components: Vec<(Component, usize, BitVector, BitVector)>,
    covers_d: bool,
}
/// ABI slot identity, independent of a function's local spelling. The domain
/// is resolved once against its subject; the same positions bind the reference.
fn interface_slot(f: &Function, name: &str) -> Result<(usize, usize)> {
    f.params
        .iter()
        .enumerate()
        .find_map(|(group, params)| {
            params
                .iter()
                .position(|p| p.name == name)
    fn domain(&self, f: &Function, d: &Json) -> Result<Domain> {
        let mut components = Vec::new();
        let mut bundles = BTreeSet::new();
        let mut regions = BTreeSet::new();
        let mut full = true;
        for (group, borrowed) in [("bundles", false), ("borrowed", true)] {
            for (ordinal, b) in d.get(group)?.array()?.iter().enumerate() {
                let name = b.get("name")?.string()?.to_string();
                let width = usize::try_from(b.get("width")?.uint()?)
                    .map_err(|_| Error::host_resource("§12.2: host limit: domain width".into()))?;
                let lo = json_bits(b.get("lo")?, width)?;
                let hi = json_bits(b.get("hi")?, width)?;
                full &= lo.is_zero() && hi == ones(width)?;
                components.push((
                    if borrowed {
                        Component::Borrowed(ordinal, name)
                    } else {
                        let (group, index) = interface_slot(f, &name)?;
                        bundles.insert((group, index));
                        Component::Bundle(group, index)
                    },
                    width,
                    lo,
                    hi,
                ));
            }
        }
        for r in d.get("regions")?.array()? {
            let name = r.get("name")?.string()?.to_string();
            let (group, slot) = interface_slot(f, &name)?;
            let (a, w) = f
                .params
                .iter()
                .flatten()
                .find_map(|p| match p.ty {
                    Type::Region(a, w) if p.name == name => Some((a, w as usize)),
                    _ => None,
                })
                .ok_or_else(|| Error::new(format!("§12.2: unknown domain region {name}")))?;
            let cells = crate::oracle::power_of_two(a as u128)
                .and_then(|n| usize::try_from(n).ok())
                .ok_or_else(|| Error::host_resource("§12.2: host limit: region cells".into()))?;
            match r.get("cells")? {
                Json::String(s) if s == "all" => {
                    regions.insert((group, slot));
                    crate::reserve_more(&mut components, cells, "§12.2", "domain components")?;
                    for i in 0..cells {
                        components.push((
                            Component::Cell(group, slot, i),
                            w,
                            BitVector::zero(w)?,
                            ones(w)?,
                        ));
                    }
                }
                cs => {
                    let mut covered = BTreeSet::new();
                    for c in cs.array()? {
                        let i = usize::try_from(c.get("index")?.uint()?)
                            .map_err(|_| Error::new("§12.2: cell index".into()))?;
                        let lo = json_bits(c.get("lo")?, w)?;
                        let hi = json_bits(c.get("hi")?, w)?;
                        full &= lo.is_zero() && hi == ones(w)?;
                        if i < cells {
                            covered.insert(i);
                        }
                        components.push((Component::Cell(group, slot, i), w, lo, hi));
                    }
                    if covered.len() == cells {
                        regions.insert((group, slot));
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // D: every interface bundle and every region in the region's input
        // domain (§5.2, §5.3; tgt/fault included for oracles).
        let groups = if f.kind == "oracle" { 4 } else { 3 };
        let covers_d = full
            && f.params[..groups]
                .iter()
                .enumerate()
                .all(|(group, params)| {
                    params.iter().enumerate().all(|(slot, p)| match p.ty {
                        Type::Region(..) => regions.contains(&(group, slot)),
                        _ => bundles.contains(&(group, slot)),
                    })
                });
        Ok(Domain {
            components,
            covers_d,
        })
    }
    /// Executes region `f` on one domain element; returns the output tuple.
    fn execute_element(
        for p in &f.params[2] {
            let (cells, w) = region_dims(p)?;
            let mut all = crate::reserved(cells, "§12.2", "action replay region cells")?;
            for i in 0..cells {
                let wires = q.allocate(w)?;
                set(&mut q, &wires, &cell(&p.name, i, w)?)?;
                all.push(wires);
            }
            let Type::Region(a, w) = p.ty else {
                return err("§5.1", "mem slot requires region type");
            };
            b.regions.insert(
                p.name.clone(),
                crate::QuantumRegion {
                    address_width: a,
                    cell_width: w,
                    cells: all,
                },
            );
        }
        let mut borrowed = Vec::new();
        for (c, v) in element {
            if let Component::Borrowed(ordinal, _) = c {
                if *ordinal != borrowed.len() {
                    return err("§12.2", "borrowed component position mismatch");
                }
                let wires = q.allocate(v.width())?;
                set(&mut q, &wires, v)?;
                borrowed.push(wires.clone());
                b.borrowed.push(wires);
            }
        }
        if audit.is_some() {
            q.start_borrow_audit()?;
        }
        self.execute_action(&f.name, &mut q, &b, false, &[])?;
        if let Some(audit) = &mut audit {
            audit.extend(q.take_borrow_audit());
        }
        let read = |q: &QuantumState, wires: &[u64]| -> Result<BitVector> {
            let mut v = BitVector::zero(wires.len())?;
            for (i, w) in wires.iter().enumerate() {
                v.set(i, q.read_basis(&[*w])? == 1)?;
            }
            Ok(v)
        };
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for p in f.params[0].iter().chain(&f.params[1]) {
            out.push(read(&q, &b.bundles[&p.name])?);
        }
        for p in &f.params[2] {
            for c in &b.regions[&p.name].cells {
                out.push(read(&q, c)?);
            }
        }
        for wires in &borrowed {
            out.push(read(&q, wires)?);
        }
        Ok(out)
    }
    fn claim_budget(&self, claim: &Json, f: &Function) -> Result<Option<Budget>> {
